## 11. Known dead schema & future considerations

A full `information_schema` audit against the live database (the schema-drift pass) surfaced schema the app does not use. Do **not** "clean these up" reactively — each is documented and intentional:

**ORPHAN AUDIT (process).** Every roadmap-touching docs commit ends by re-running the orphan check — read §11 end to end and list any finding or backlog item with **no commit ref, no C### designation, and no Tier/roadmap line** — and stating the result. A finding with nowhere to go is a finding that will be lost.

> **2026-08-30 run — CLEAN BUT FOR ONE, AND THE ONE WAS THE PREDICTED KIND.** Mechanised this time rather than read by eye: **all 50 `O###` ids in §11 have a ROADMAP row · all 48 `C###` refs have a real commit · 60 of 61 top-level bullets carry an id, a commit or a tier line.** The single orphan was **cold-start categorization**, held only by *"the two items above JOINTLY own it"* — **a cross-reference, which the 2026-08-08 run already recorded as not an anchor.** Two years of that lesson and it recurred in the same form, which is the argument for running this as a script rather than a habit: **a human sweep finds items with NOTHING; only a mechanical one reliably finds items whose anchor is a sentence pointing elsewhere.**

> **2026-08-08 run — five items stamped.** The check caught three bullets carrying no anchor at all (bulk soft-delete/void UI · trust-panel Documents glyph · Home Activity feed jargon) and two anchored only by cross-reference from OTHER bullets (business-type COA · confidence calibration). **All five were already on the roadmap** — TIER 2 and TIER 1 #6/#7 respectively — so nothing had actually been lost; the divergence was one-directional, the roadmap knowing what §11 didn't say. **Lesson: "is it scheduled?" must be answerable from the item itself, not by grepping for a sibling that happens to mention it.** Same failure shape the security cluster's Tier 1 designation would have had — §11 calling it launch-blocking while the TIER 1 list omitted it (fixed in the same pass, now ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #11).

- **Backlog — bulk-selection soft-delete / void UI in Books (not built). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 "bulk soft-delete/void UI".** `softDeleteInvoices` (plural, batch + single Undo toast) exists in `ERP` but is wired to **no component** — the UI exposes only per-entry delete/void. Justified by the O83 duplicate incident: remediating the 14 double-booked entries required scripted DB access (soft-delete + audit rows via the Management API) because there's no in-app way to select and remove a batch. Build a multi-select + bulk soft-delete/void surface in Books (route through the existing `softDeleteInvoices`/`voidInvoiceWithUndo` so audit + Undo are preserved) so batch remediation is self-serve.

- **★ NORTH STAR — bank statements are HANDLED, not OPERATED (not built; sequence after launch-gate).** *(Supersedes and absorbs the former "bank-import verdict" backlog item and the universal-drop-zone note.)* Bank Import and Reconcile are bookkeeper-workbench surfaces inherited from the QBO mental model; **Shadow's client persona has no bookkeeper role**, so a client dropped into raw line-lists + checkboxes + "Needs review" / "Select all" is being handed a job that isn't theirs (O83 live-drive: the operator nearly re-booked 20 duplicates via Select-All three separate times, despite writing the fixture himself). **Target architecture:** uploading a statement from ANY surface — including the home drop zone (whose promise "your AI controller handles the rest" today delivers a multi-screen manual flow) — triggers an **automatic pipeline with zero required interaction on the clean path**: parse → dedupe against books (idempotency guard, `markAlreadyBooked`) → book new lines → match booked lines (A/P matcher) → derive opening/ending balances from the statement → complete a **verified** reconciliation. The client sees **outcomes, not tasks**: "January matched to your bank ✓" on the trust panel + plain-language confirmation of anything auto-booked. **Exceptions** (unmatched lines, balance discrepancies, low-confidence categorizations) route to the **CPA Review queue**; the client is only ever asked plain-language questions the CPA can't resolve. **Bank Import and Reconcile demote to read-only history / exception views for the CPA — never a required client workflow.** Nearly all the machinery already exists post-O83 (idempotent re-parse, deterministic A/P auto-matching, statement-derived opening/ending balances, verified-balance completion + honest gate records that don't false-pass); the **remaining work is orchestration + the exception queue** (and the `bank_account_id` linkage below, which lets the pipeline persist/source statement lines instead of re-parsing). Interim mitigation already shipped: C174 truthful already-booked labeling in Bank Import (so the current manual surface at least can't mislead into re-booking).

  **★ NORTH STAR PHASE 2 — IA COLLAPSE (not built; sequence AFTER Phase 1 pipeline automation).** Once the clean-path pipeline runs end-to-end (parse→book→match→reconcile automatically, Phase 1 above), collapse the navigation to match the role model. **Client-facing nav = Home (drop zone + trust panel + business health), Reports, and a read-only Activity/Documents view — nothing else.** Everything **workbench-shaped** (Bank Import, Reconcile, Matching Engine, Payables-as-a-workflow, Payroll import) either **demotes to CPA-only surfaces behind the existing reviewer role**, or becomes **pipeline internals surfaced as OUTCOMES on Home** ("January matched to your bank ✓"), never as tabs the client operates. **Evidence (O83):** the operator *himself* repeatedly couldn't locate the Matching Engine (reachable only via a post-booking redirect), stalled on Bank Import's checkbox states, and had to hand-type a statement balance the parser had already extracted — the ten-sub-tab Books nav is **QBO's bookkeeper IA transplanted onto a product whose client persona has no bookkeeper**. **Sequencing is strict: pipeline automation FIRST (the tabs become UNNECESSARY), THEN IA collapse (the tabs become GONE)** — collapsing the nav before the pipeline can do the work would just hide functions the client still needs. **The reviewer role built for sign-off separation (C178/O83) is the SAME gate that declutters the client:** every surface gated to `isReviewer` disappears from the client's view for free — the separation-of-duties boundary and the IA-simplification boundary are one and the same. This is the UI expression of Phase 1's "outcomes, not tasks."

- **★ O84 PART 1 — COMPLETE (2026-08-04 live drive of the persisted-statement pipeline, C185–C187; remediated by C188–C191).** ✅ **RESULT: March 2026 completed live** — 22 lines: **13 auto-booked** + **5 human-resolved** (low-confidence exceptions, surfaced only after C191) + the **Atlas outstanding clear** (no double-book) + **2 A/P clears** + **2 payroll matches**; **auto-reconciliation completed at 27,762.31 / 27,762.31 / 0.00** against an **independent answer key**; **line idempotency held across 5 statement uploads**. **Part 2 (April dirty-path fixture: forced exceptions, attention flow, CPA resolution) is the remaining O84 scope, sequenced after C192–C193.**
  **RESOLVED this drive:** payroll parse died on trailing text → **C188** (`extractFirstJson`, + a 19-site sweep list for deliberate migration, **DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §2 Bugs `O99`**) · payroll `payment_status` `ap_tie` leak → **C189** (+ the §9 cash-settled-write anti-pattern) · planner dead-zone guarantee + review-screen teardown → **C190** (exhaustiveness invariant + conditional `bankStep`) · **planner-exception id seam — THE actual live five-pending-lines bug** (exceptions carried the parse-time local id, so the DB write matched zero rows AND the lines were filtered out of the review table: invisible in the DB *and* the UI) → **C191**, mutation-tested (3 of 5 new tests proven red pre-fix).
  Original six findings:
  1. **STATEMENT/DOCUMENT DEDUP (→ C193).** Re-uploading the same statement creates a NEW `bank_statements` row + a NEW `documents` row each time (verified live: **3× Feb rows**). Line-level idempotency HELD — **zero double-books** (`markAlreadyBooked` / `bankTxnKey`) — but the `bank_statements` table and the doc library accumulate duplicates. **Fix:** a **SHA-256 content hash at `storeDocument` time, unique per company** — identical bytes link to the existing document instead of inserting; `bank_statements` dedups on **account + period + content hash**, superseding the prior row.
  2. **QUEUE COPY SHOWS THE PRE-OVERRIDE AI LABEL.** The processing queue said "booked … as a client meal" while the entry correctly booked to **5000 COGS** via the learned vendor mapping. The displayed label must be the **FINAL post-mapping account**, not the raw AI guess — an owner-facing honesty bug. Live specimen of the confidence/reasoning-consistency item (Tier 1 #7) — **fold into that work**, not a new entry.
  3. **PAYROLL INGESTION FORMAT GAP.** The Home drop zone advertises PDF, but payroll accepts only `csv/xls/xlsx/iif/txt` — a **Gusto PDF summary** (the artifact an owner actually has) is rejected. Either **parse payroll PDFs through the extraction path** or **stop advertising what we reject**. **DESIGNATED: its own ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 line** ("payroll PDF ingestion through the extraction path") — it was previously mis-filed under the doc-library line, where ingestion work would have been lost.
  4. **DOC LIBRARY DATE = UPLOAD DATE.** Cards show the **Aug upload date for a Feb statement**. Same as the existing doc-library backlog item **(c)** (derived document date) — the date must be the derived statement/invoice date; **now confirmed live**, fold into that item.
  5. **DOCS SEARCH MISSING.** The library header says "stored and searchable" with **no search input**. Minimum: **filename + type + date-range**; content search later on extraction text. Folds into the Tier 2 doc-library work.
  6. **API CREDIT EXHAUSTION HAS NO GRACEFUL MODE.** The org ran out of Anthropic credits mid-drive; extraction-dependent features fail **without a clear in-app explanation**. Launch-week: **credit/balance monitoring, auto-reload guidance, and an explicit degraded-mode banner** ("document reading is paused") instead of silent failures.

  **Back-half findings (added after the drive's second session):**
  - **(a) OPENING-BALANCE BANNER IS OUTSTANDING-BLIND.** It warns on a bank-vs-books difference (**$275 live**) that the **reconciliation chain fully explains** — the Atlas uncleared check. It should consult `priorOutstandingCandidates` (C187) and render **"explained by N uncleared item(s) ✓"** instead of an alarm. **Small, high trust-value** — the same false-alarm class as the C179 comparand fix: frightening a client with a difference the system already understands.
  - **(b) TRANSITIONAL REVIEW FLASH.** On upload the **full parsed line list renders briefly**, then the pipeline's verdict replaces it — content appears, then vanishes. Replace with a **"handling this automatically…"** state that resolves *directly* into the outcome summary (never render a list the client isn't meant to operate). **Dissolves fully at IA collapse (North Star Phase 2); cheap interim fix.**
  - **(c) ★ SILENT WRITES — the root-cause CLASS of the night (→ C192, NEXT CODE COMMIT).** Supabase updates that match **zero rows still "succeed"** (no error), and empty `catch {}` blocks swallowed **two live bugs** this drive (C189's missing stamp and C191's id seam were both invisible for exactly this reason). **Fix:** a **checked-write helper** for row-targeted mutations — assert **no error AND rows-affected ≥ 1**, with a **loud console warning + a visible failure counter** on violation; **adopt at every `bank_statement_lines` / `journal_entries` mutation site**. Plus **§9 doctrine**: ids reach DB calls **only** through `lineDbId` (grep-enforceable), and **every new write path stamps the hygiene fields** (`payment_status`, `bank_account_id`, `meta.kind`).
  - **(d) STATEMENT/DOCUMENT CONTENT-HASH DEDUP (→ C193).** The live library now holds **3× March + 3× Feb copies**. Dedup per finding 1's spec: **SHA-256 at `storeDocument`** (unique per company; identical bytes link to the existing document) and **statement supersede on account + period + hash**.

- **★ O84 PART 2 — COMPLETE (2026-08-05, the April dirty-path drive). O84 IS NOW FULLY COMPLETE — 4 attested months.** ✅ **RESULT: April 2026 driven end-to-end and SIGNED OFF.** The pipeline correctly landed **'attention'** (**no false reconciliation** — the honest outcome for a dirty month). Of the 3 designed traps: the **mystery deposit** was **excepted** (routed to a human, as designed) and the **merchant fees** were **legitimately BEATEN** — booked to **6520 at 99 confidence with coherent reasoning**, meaning **the §11 miscategorization specimen no longer reproduces**. Manual reconcile completed at **33,064.41 / 32,714.41 / 0** with **check #1043 written to `outstanding_books`** — **THE CHAIN PRODUCED ITS FIRST ITEM** (May will consume it, exercising C187's clear path on a self-generated item). **Learning layer, precisely observed:** Lone Star (taught) → **booked at 87**; Bluebonnet (taught) → **excepted at 78** — i.e. the learned mapping informed the **SUGGESTION but not the SCORE**. **Line idempotency and ledger integrity held throughout; ZERO wrong entries across the whole of O84.**

  **PART 2 FINDINGS:**
  - **★★ FALSE ATTESTATION SUCCESS (→ C194, NEXT CODE COMMIT — before anything else).** `ReconView`'s completion renders **"Your books match your bank ✓" UNGATED**: a failed completion insert is merely `console.warn`'d and **the success screen renders anyway** — **reproduced live with NO row existing**. This is the **worst class of bug in this product** (a false green on the attestation surface itself). **Trigger isolated by A/B:** entering the balance **BEFORE** resolving the outstanding item made the session autosave fail; completion then **UPDATED a nonexistent row** — a **zero-row, UNCHECKED** write (ReconView sites were **deferred** in the C192 sweep — this is that debt coming due). **C194 scope:** root-cause the ordering failure; **gate the ✓ on a VERIFIED complete row** (checked write **+ re-select**); adopt `checkedRowUpdate`/`checkedIdsUpdate` at **all** ReconView mutation sites; **regression test pinning "completion write failure → NO success state, plain-language 'nothing was locked in'".**
  - **★ CONFIDENCE PRICES DESCRIPTOR CLARITY, NOT VENDOR FAMILIARITY (Tier 1 #7 spec input — the sharpest specimen yet).** A **never-seen** vendor auto-booked **$850 at 88** on mere *name plausibility*, while a **twice-taught** vendor scored **78 and excepted**. The score is reading **descriptor legibility**, not **what we actually know about this client's vendors**. **Requirement:** **novel vendors DAMPENED below the auto-book floor; taught vendors BOOSTED above it** — confidence must price familiarity. (Pairs with the onboarding vendor teach-in / entity-profiles item: teaching is worthless if it doesn't move the score.) **MAY EVIDENCE (O85) — the score WOBBLES:** Lone Star, taught and **booked @87 in April**, was **excepted below the floor in May** — with the correct taught account already pre-selected. The mapping is stable month-to-month; the CONFIDENCE isn't. So calibration must not merely nudge taught vendors up once — it must **stabilize them above the floor across periods**, or the same vendor keeps oscillating between auto-booked and hand-resolved for no reason the client can see.
  - **→ C195 — WORKBENCH HONESTY SWEEP** (the narration layer, batched): **(1)** exception cards **not refreshed after MANUAL booking** (**5 zombies live** — the C193 supersede fix's sibling on the manual path); **(2)** reconciliation completion **doesn't retire covered statements'** attention status/cards (**1 stale card persists post-sign-off**); **(3)** **anomaly pattern suppression** — an identical 4-card Bluebonnet dismissal **two months running** is alarm fatigue (see the §11 pattern-level duplicate-suppression item); **(4)** **manual JE entry is UNREACHABLE** — `AddView` is orphaned, so booking check #1043 required **direct SQL mirroring `post_journal_entry`** (**near-Tier-1**: a CPA cannot hand-enter a transaction); **(5)** **transitional parse flash** (now raised **4×**); **(6)** booking toast reported **0/0 after booking 5**; **(7)** **intake orphan should auto-resolve** on content-hash match to an already-recorded doc (C193's hash, applied to the intake ledger); **(8)** **outstanding-check copy must pass the "knows nothing" bar.** **STANDING DIRECTIVE (record once, applies everywhere): every owner-facing surface assumes ZERO accounting knowledge** — this generalizes the Cardinal Principle from "no jargon" to "no assumed concepts."

- **★ O85 — MAY DRIVE, IN FLIGHT (→ C196, honesty follow-ups).** The May statement consumes April's self-generated outstanding item (check #1043), exercising C187's clear path end-to-end for the first time. Findings so far:
  - **(a) BANK IMPORT'S OPENING BANNER IS STILL THE ORANGE ALARM.** C195 item 8 gave **Reconcile** the calm, chain-aware copy ("explained by 1 check ✓"), but **Bank Import's** banner was not touched — so the same fully-explained gap still reads as a warning on the surface the client actually lands on first. **Fix:** adopt `openingMismatchCopy` + `priorOutstandingCandidates` there too. (Same false-alarm class as C179; the fix already exists, it just isn't wired to both surfaces.)
  - **(b) POST-PIPELINE SUMMARY TILES NARRATE ONLY THE RESIDUE.** After a 21-line statement runs, the tiles read **"Total transactions 5"** — the count of what's LEFT, not what happened. The counters must describe the WHOLE statement: **"21 transactions · 16 handled automatically · 5 need your input."** **That sentence IS the product pitch** — the one moment the client sees the machine's whole contribution — and today it's hidden behind a number that makes the product look like it did less than nothing. Surface it prominently, not as a footnote.
  - **(c) ANOMALY DETECTORS FIRE ON PAYROLL/SYSTEM ENTRIES.** The large-charge and round-amount detectors flagged a **$4,000 payroll gross** as "may need to be capitalized" — nonsense for a payroll leg. **Fix:** exempt `source=payroll` (and system kinds generally) from `large_transaction` and `round_number`. Detection should never opine on entries whose shape it doesn't own.
  - **(d) RECONCILE NEEDS "ABANDON THIS MATCH".** An accidentally-started match session left an `open` reconciliation row that required **manual SQL** to clean up — there is no in-app way to discard a session you didn't mean to start. **Fix:** an explicit, confirm-guarded abandon action that deletes/retires the open row (routed through the C192 checked writes + an audit row), so no reconciliation ever needs a DBA.

- **★ O86 — JUNE DRIVE, COMPLETE (2026-08-06; → C198). MONTH SIX SIGNED OFF.** *(Outcome: **(a)(g)(i)(j)(k)(l) SHIPPED + live-verified** — see ★ O86·2 below; **(b)(c)(d)(e)(f) — ALL SHIPPED**: (b) → C198·3a `68151c5`; (c)(d)(e)(f) → C198·3b `30cf680`. **C198 IS COMPLETE.** *(Read "(b)(c)(d)(e)(f) remain" until the 2026-08-11 sweep — this section stamped (e) and (f) shipped elsewhere while this summary still asked for them.)*)* The June drive ran on the post-C197 client seat (Home + Reports), so for the first time the findings are about what the OWNER experiences rather than what the workbench exposes. ✅ **RESULT: June 2026 reconciled and signed off** — reconciliation **46,434.91 / 46,434.91 · difference 0 · `complete` · verified · `outstanding_books` []**, the `bank_statements` row reached **`complete`**, and the month holds **exactly two Gusto entries** (no payroll duplication). **Operator rubric:** 1 — No · 2 — No · 3 — **upload flow not clean** · 4 — No · 5 — **"Not yet, but closer."** The engine and the ledger were right again; **the drive was spent fighting the INTAKE surface**, which is precisely what C198 (a) exists to remove. Findings:
  - **(a) ★★ HEADLINE — DROP-ZONE AUTO-RUN (single-account).** The Home drop zone still only **stashes** a statement for the CPA ("your accountant will add these") — account binding was deferred at C186 (the offset account can't be read off the file) and never revisited, and C197 turned that deferral into the client's whole experience. **Fix:** a company with exactly **ONE bank account auto-binds**, so a drop on Home runs the **FULL pipeline** (parse → dedupe → book → match → derive balances → reconcile) and the **trust panel narrates the outcome**. This is the **missing half of "handled, not operated"** — the North Star's clean path has existed since C186 but nothing client-facing could reach it. **Related fragility:** the pending-import stash is **in-memory** — today's Act-1 statement stash was **lost before Bank Import could consume it**. Auto-run **replaces** the stash for the single-account case; the stash must be **persisted** for multi-account companies (where a human still picks the account).
  - **(b) PAYROLL AUTO-POST — PROMOTED (Tier 3 → C198-adjacent / Tier 1-adjacent).** A CPA clicking **"Post to Ledger"** on a standard biweekly register is **operating, not reviewing** — the same class of defect as (a), one surface over. **Fix:** **standard-shape registers auto-post** with an **undo window + audit row**; the debit/credit confirm card survives **only for non-standard shapes**. (Supersedes the Tier 3 "payroll auto-post" product idea below, which was scoped as post-launch polish before the client seat made it load-bearing.)
  - **(c) `firstUnsignedMonth` SPEC FIX (Act 0).** The C196 helper picks the earliest month **with activity** that isn't signed off; the correct rule is **the month AFTER the latest sign-off, independent of activity** — a quiet month is still the month to review next, and skipping it silently walks the reviewer past a period nobody attested. **Fix + test:** a **zero-activity unsigned month** case (activity list empty, prior month signed → returns the following month).
  - **(d) OWNER-SURFACE SELF-CONTRADICTION.** The trust panel says **"Nothing needs your attention"** while an **amber "5 unusual patterns"** box sits **below it on the same screen** — the panel and the box are both telling the truth about different things and the owner reads a contradiction. **Fix:** on the **owner seat**, LOW anomalies render as **at most one muted line (or nothing at all)**; **amber/warning framing is CPA-side only**. (The C197 seat boundary is the mechanism; this is the content decision it enables.)
  - **(e) BURN/RUNWAY CONTRADICTION — PROMOTED onto C198.** *"You're profitable **with ~2 months of runway** · Needs attention"* now renders on the **demo surface** a prospect sees, which moves it from a backlog honesty bug to a launch-facing one. Resolve per the original Tier 1 #9 spec (burn = **net cash change**; profitable/cash-positive → **no runway warning**, positive framing). See the §11 burn/runway backlog item for the blast-radius caveat — one shared figure across card, drill and AI snapshot.
  - **(f) ANOMALIES AUTO-EXPIRE ON SIGN-OFF — ✅ SHIPPED (C198·3b f1, `30cf680`; migration `060`).** Open **LOW/MEDIUM** anomalies whose period gets **signed off** should **auto-resolve** — *"period attested over this note"* — instead of lingering into later months. A reviewer who attested the month has, by that act, judged its low-severity notes acceptable; carrying them forward is the alarm-fatigue class again (cf. the pattern-level duplicate-suppression item). HIGH anomalies are deliberately excluded — those should **block** sign-off, not be retired by it.
  - **(g) SPREADSHEET MIS-ROUTE (the C100 caveat, suspected live).** The universal drop path classifies **ALL spreadsheets as bank statements** (`docType = "bank_statement"` for any unrecognized spreadsheet), so a **payroll CSV dropped on Home** never reaches payroll intake. **Verify against the live path and fix**, with a `fileDetect` test pinning payroll-CSV → payroll intake. Pairs with (a): auto-run is only safe if the router is right about what it's auto-running.
  - **(h) FUTURE (note, NOT C198) — Reconcile collapses to an exceptions-only surface.** Once confidence calibration lands (Tier 1 #7) and the auto-run pipeline of (a) is carrying the clean path, **Reconcile stops being a standing tab** and becomes an **exceptions view the CPA visits only when the pipeline couldn't finish** — the same demotion C197 gave the client, applied to the reviewer once the machine earns it. *(Carried on ROADMAP §0 TIER 3.)*
  - **★★ DESIGN DECISION — THE MACHINE COMPLETES THE RECONCILIATION (Alex, 2026-08-06; shipped C198·2).** *Verbatim:* **"when the pipeline completes and the balance ties, the MACHINE completes the reconciliation. Rationale: lower tiers have reduced/no CPA-review cadence; the pipeline must function without a CPA click. Reconciliation = arithmetic (machine-verifiable). Sign-off = judgment (human, always). C194's gate applies unchanged: a reconciliation row may only be created complete when the balance verifiably ties — auto-complete converts a proven tie into a record, never manufactures one."** Implemented as `shouldAutoCompleteReconciliation` (all lines settled AND balance ties AND no completed reconciliation already covers the period) re-asserting `canCompleteReconciliation` immediately before the insert, writing through the EXISTING `completePipelineReconciliation` (C186) — no second writer, no new column: the **auto-vs-manual marker lives in the audit log** (`auto: true` + a plain-language sentence). **No tie → no row, ever** (mutation-tested).
  - **(g) VERIFIED — ALREADY FIXED (O55), NOW PINNED (C198·2).** The xlsx mis-route was **not reproducible**: `detectFileType` has sniffed binary workbooks through the xlsx lib since O55, and it was **verified live 2026-08-06 against real Gusto and bank workbooks**. What was missing was a **test holding it there** — every prior `fileDetect` test feeds TEXT, so the entire binary branch (`arrayBuffer` → `XLSX.read` → `sheet_to_json` → `detectFromText`) was unpinned. **Deliverable: `tests/xlsxRouting.test.js`** builds REAL .xlsx bytes in memory with the repo's own xlsx lib and asserts through the real async detector — payroll register (named AND neutrally-named), bank export, QBO export, junk sheet, corrupt bytes. One-line detector improvement went with it: garbage that xlsx TOLERATES (rather than throwing) now returns an explicit "no readable rows" reason instead of an unknown carrying no reason at all; routing is unchanged.
  - **(i) SECOND HALF CLOSED (C198·2) — the INTAKE row advances too.** C198·1 narrowed and fixed the `bank_statements` status advance; the **intake ledger** had the same defect — a statement whose every line was in the books stayed `held_for_review` forever, so the completeness net kept describing finished work as parked. Now the intake row advances **HELD → RECORDED with its journal-entry linkage** (generalizing C196(5)'s payroll path) exactly when all lines are settled, and stays HELD when any line is open. **No backfill — May's dangling rows stay as evidence.**
  - **(i) NARROWED — statement status only advances on the RECONCILE-COMPLETION path.** The **first-pass pipeline** path fails to advance `bank_statements` rows past `held_for_review`/`attention`; the **reconcile-completion** path DOES advance them (proved live: the **June row reached `complete`** while the **May rows still dangle**). So this is not "status is broken" — it is one missing transition on one path. **Fix scope: the first-pass path ONLY** (don't touch the completion path that demonstrably works).
  - **(j) ★★ HEADLINE BUG (June drive) — RE-UPLOAD OF A NON-COMPLETE STATEMENT SILENTLY NO-OPS.** Re-uploading a statement with an **identical content hash** onto a **non-complete** statement is swallowed by the C193 dedupe: it does **not** re-run the pipeline and does **not** re-evaluate the reconciliation. The stale **'attention'** status then **lies to Review** — the screen reports a problem the books no longer have. **Worse, the surface then demands work the system doesn't need:** the **Reconcile tab starts EMPTY** and asks for a **third physical upload plus a hand-typed ending balance**, when the system already holds the **parsed statement** (`bank_statements.stated_ending_balance`) **and every booked line**. **Required behavior:** (1) **once all statement lines are booked, reconciliation is OFFERED directly** — no re-upload, no re-parse; (2) **dedupe on a NON-COMPLETE statement RE-EVALUATES** (re-run pipeline / re-derive status) instead of no-oping. Dedupe must stay a no-op only where the statement is genuinely **finished**. *(This is the same "don't make the client re-do what we already did" class as (a), on the CPA side.)*
  - **(k) REVIEW'S STATEMENT-LEVEL EXCEPTION CARD LINKS NOWHERE USEFUL.** The card's **"Open in Bank Import"** renders an **empty screen** when the lines are already booked — the link's target is line-level, but the exception is **statement-level**. **Fix:** a target that carries the context — **Reconcile**, or a **statement detail view** — so the reviewer lands on the thing the card is actually about. *(Sibling of the §11 "Review-queue anomaly cards lack inspection affordances" item — same defect shape, different card.)*
  - **(l) RECONCILE MUST PRE-FILL THE ENDING BALANCE from `bank_statements.stated_ending_balance`.** The value is **already persisted** (C185), yet the completion bar still makes the CPA **hand-type a number the system holds**. **Fix:** prefill from the stored statement, **field stays editable** — it remains the CPA's **independent check**, not a rubber stamp. *(Promotes the §11 "Reconcile's Bank Ending Balance field should PREFILL" backlog item onto C198, and now sources it from the PERSISTED statement rather than a re-parse.)*
  - **(m) NOTE ONLY — DISMISSAL-WITH-REASON AS A LEARNING SIGNAL (spec belongs to the calibration session, Tier 1 #7; NOT C198).** When a reviewer dismisses a card with a user-confirmed reason ("recurring charge"), that context should **suppress future cards of the same shape** AND **seed a recurring-rule suggestion** — the dismissal is real human knowledge and today it evaporates. **Hard constraint: it must NEVER silently alter booking behavior** — a dismissal explains a flag, it does not re-code an entry; any booking change stays an explicit, visible action. *(Pairs with the pattern-level duplicate-suppression item and `learnFromCorrection`.)*

  **Also recorded from the June drive:**
  - **STALENESS DETECTOR USES `now()` INSTEAD OF PERIOD CONTEXT (→ C198, with the (d)/(f) anomaly work).** "Vendor hasn't charged you in **51 / 52 / 59 days**" rendered **on the same screen as that vendor's current-month charge** — the detector measures against **wall-clock**, not the period the books are in. This fires wrongly for **ANY books trailing wall-clock, i.e. every real client** (bookkeeping is always behind). **Fix:** measure staleness against the **period context** (latest booked/attested period), never `now()`.
  - **ANOMALY CARDS DOUBLE-EMITTED ON STATEMENT RE-UPLOAD (→ C198, same batch).** A re-upload took the queue from **5 → 10** cards: the C193 dedupe covers **lines**, not **anomaly emission**. **Fix:** anomaly emission must be idempotent per (fingerprint, period) across re-runs — the fingerprint machinery exists (C178), it just isn't consulted on the re-upload path.
  - **MULTI-COMPANY v1 JUNK CENSUS (quarantined; cleanup deferred → ROADMAP §0 TIER 3).** The **fixture company `3a704760` (Franklin Ave Pizza LLC) is FULLY CLEAN — zero pre-July entries**, so no O83–O86 result is contaminated. Surviving v1/demo junk lives in **`c787582f` (Test Corp ONE — 16 entries incl. personal purchases; the Dell asset + Bob's contact were retired 2026-08-06)**, **`24d5e576` (Northwind Studio — 21)**, **`3a4a6b3c` (Shadow Test Co — 12)**, **`66fa0cc2` (1)**. All of it is **quarantined by company scoping** (RLS + `company_id`), so it cannot leak into a real tenant; cleanup is **deferred, not forgotten** — pair it with the Tier 2 demo/showcase-company work so the demo entity is built clean rather than cleaned up.

  - **(a) DROP-ZONE AUTO-RUN + AUTO-RECONCILE — LIVE-VERIFIED.** An owner-seat drop of the June statement ran the whole pipeline unattended and rendered **"All 21 transactions from your statement are in your books, and this month was already checked against your bank ✓"** — a **machine-verified** sentence: `alreadyReconciled` is derived from **real coverage** (`reconciliationCoversStatement`), never inferred from `ready` (which is false for two different reasons), so the clause cannot appear where the coverage fact isn't actually known.   - **QUEUE-LINE HONESTY (C198·2b).** The tile said **"Done"** beside the **old stash sentence** over work already booked: the (a1) branch stamped `done` **before** awaiting `handleBankFile`, and nothing wrote the outcome back. Now: **processing → await → done-with-outcome**, a throw stamps **"Needs a look"** and never `done`, the stash sentence is **structurally unreachable** for a `to:"pipeline"` result, and an in-flight run says **"Adding these to your books…"**.
  - **★ C195(7) HAD SHIPPED INERT — REVIVED (`c2dcbbc`).** The duplicate auto-resolve **never fired once**: `reconcileIntake` built its dropped rows without `content_hash`, so `reconcileDroppedDocs`' `hashes` array was **always empty** and the whole block was unreachable. **A block that never runs looks identical to a block with nothing to do** — which is why it went unnoticed from C195 to now. Fixed by carrying `content_hash` + `document_id` through, and **live-verified clearing a 2-day-old orphan on the next Review load**, with plain-language detail. *(Lesson worth generalizing: a guard whose input is always empty is indistinguishable from a clean queue — prefer asserting the mechanism FIRES, not merely that nothing is outstanding.)*
  - **NOTE — THE UPLOAD QUEUE IS IN-MEMORY. DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §2 Bugs `O97`** *(was "pursue later, not scheduled", which anchors nothing — it carries live data-loss evidence and now carries a row).* `uploadQueue` + `fileStoreRef` are React state/ref, so a **reload or navigation between enqueue and processing wipes an arrival** before it is ever processed. **Live evidence:** the **2026-08-06 20:11:42** orphan — the operator hard-refreshed between drops, and the intake row sat at `received` while the same bytes were dropped again 23s later. Mitigations that now exist: the revived auto-resolve **self-heals the already-recorded-bytes case**, and a genuinely lost file still surfaces through the **completeness net after 30 minutes**. The real fix is a **durable queue** (persist the pending work, not just the arrival record) — **not scheduled**.
  - **CALIBRATION FOOTNOTE — auto-reconcile DECLINES months with outstanding items.** The auto path computes its difference with a **hardcoded zero outstanding**, so any month carrying an uncleared item fails to tie and correctly falls back to the **offered CPA session**. That is deliberately conservative (never assert a tie the arithmetic didn't prove). **Whether the outstanding chain should ever feed the AUTO path is a calibration-session question** — it is a judgment about how much the machine may assume, not a bug.
  - **COPY NIT (→ C198·3).** Bank Import's re-upload toast says **"21 transactions imported"** when all 21 were **deduped** — it should say we **already have** them. Same class as the queue-line fix above, one surface over.

- **★ O87 — JULY DRIVE, COMPLETE (2026-08-09; → C198·3c). MONTH SEVEN SIGNED OFF — THE MAGIC PATH'S FIRST LIVE FIRE.** ✅ **RESULT: July 2026 reconciled and signed off — `54,880.41 / 54,880.41 · difference 0 · complete · verified · outstanding_books []`, AUTO-COMPLETED by the pipeline on exception booking.** This is the **first live auto-reconcile**: the machine converted a proven tie into a record with no CPA click, exactly as the C198·2 design decision specified. **17 of 21 lines auto-booked** — including **Lone Star, which auto-booked for the first time after flapping UPWARD past the floor following two sub-floor months** (calibration evidence, see (iv)). **C198·3b's f1 made its debut: 16 notes closed as `attested`/`2026-07` at sign-off.** Findings:
  - **(i) ★★ HEADLINE — THE PAYROLL AUTO-POST GATE IS STRUCTURALLY INERT (shipped C198·3a, never able to fire).** `payrollHistoryFromLedger` selects on `import_metadata.kind === 'payroll'`; **no payroll entry has ever carried `import_metadata`**, so history is **always empty**, so **condition 5 (within-norms) fails for every company, forever**. Both July registers correctly fell to the confirm card — the gate **failed CLOSED**, which is the one mercy: an inert gate that defaults to "ask a human" is a missed feature, not a wrong posting.
    - **★ ROOT CAUSE IS ONE LAYER DEEPER THAN "the poster doesn't stamp it" — and it is NOT payroll-specific.** The poster stamps `meta` correctly and `persistMultiLineEntry` passes it as `p_meta`. **`post_journal_entry` (migration `010`) then cherry-picks SIX named scalar keys out of `p_meta`** (`ai_reasoning`, `ai_confidence`, `approval_status`, `payment_status`, `payment_method`, `due_date`) **into their own columns and never writes `import_metadata` at all.** Every other key — `kind`, `gross`, `net`, `withholdings`, `employer_taxes`, `period` — is silently discarded at the RPC boundary. Since the RPC is the **single canonical write path** (§7), **every entry posted through it has `import_metadata = NULL`**; the only writers are the payment path's follow-up `.update()` (App.jsx:6087) and the QBO direct insert. **⚠ This means the fix "stamp it on post" DOES NOT WORK if stamped into `p_meta`** — it must be either a follow-up `checkedRowUpdate` (the shape the payment path already uses, **no migration**) or a change to `post_journal_entry` (**a migration**, which would move `·3c` off "migrations unchanged"). Decide which before writing code.
    - **★ BLAST RADIUS beyond payroll — other `import_metadata.kind` readers are suspect on the same evidence.** `matchPayrollBankLine` (`payroll.js`) keys on `kind==='payroll'`, so **O72's payroll bank-line reconciliation is inert by the same mechanism** — a payroll net-pay bank line cannot match its register. `depreciation.js` reads `{kind:'depreciation', asset_id, period}` and needs the same check. **NOT affected:** `isSystemPostedEntry` (C196(4) noise exemption) also tests `source`, which the RPC *does* write, so it still works; `apBackfill`/`settlementLink` read the payment path's kinds, which are stamped by that path's own update. **Audit every `import_metadata` reader as part of ·3c** — one null column has been quietly disabling features for as long as the RPC has existed.
    - **★★ WHY THE TESTS PASSED — the C195(7) lesson, in test form.** `tests/payrollAutoPostGate.test.js` fed the gate **synthetic history shaped to match what the extractor reads**, so extractor and fixture agreed with each other while neither agreed with the database. There was **no ROUND-TRIP test** — post → read back → extract → gate — asserting that **the poster's actual output is readable by the consumer**. A gate that never fires and a gate with nothing to do are indistinguishable from a green suite. **Mandatory in ·3c: the round-trip test**, and treat "both sides of a contract tested against the same fixture" as a test smell wherever it appears.
    - **★ DECIDED (2026-08-09) — FIX VIA FOLLOW-UP CHECKED UPDATE, NOT AN RPC CHANGE.** Stamp `{kind, gross, pay_date, period}` with a **`checkedRowUpdate` issued after `post_journal_entry` returns** — the pattern the payment path already uses (**App.jsx:6087 precedent**), so **·3c stays MIGRATION-FREE** (ledger unchanged, through `061`). Plus **one-off backfill SQL for the 14 existing Gusto JEs** so the norm has history from day one, and the copy fix above.
    - **RPC CONSOLIDATION IS A SEPARATE, DELIBERATE ITEM — not a bug-fix rider.** Making `import_metadata` a first-class `post_journal_entry` parameter is the right end state (it removes the two-write window and the whole class of "the RPC silently dropped my key"), but it changes the single canonical write path for **every** caller, and that does not belong bolted onto a payroll fix. **Recorded as future work; NOT scheduled — but no longer unanchored: DESIGNATED ROADMAP §2 Bugs `O95`** ("not scheduled" is the opposite of a home; the 2026-08-11 sweep flagged it).
    - **★★ TESTING — THE UNIT TEST CANNOT REACH THE BUG, SO THE DRIVE SCRIPT MUST.** The round-trip test asserts the **client contract** (post → stamp update issued → extractor reads it back). That is necessary and still **insufficient**: it runs on the client side of a boundary this bug lived on the far side of. **So the next drive's script gains a LIVE BOUNDARY CHECK — after the first August register posts, SQL-verify `import_metadata IS NOT NULL` on the new JE. DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #12.** Unit tests cannot cross the DB boundary, and that is *precisely* how this escaped a green suite for a whole release.
  - **(ii) STATEMENT PERIOD DERIVED FROM TRANSACTION SPAN, NOT THE STATED PERIOD.** July parsed as **07-01 → 07-27** (last txn) against a **stated period end of 07-31**. Harmless in July because the tie still proved, but the span is an *inference* where the statement carries the *fact*. **Fix: prefer the STATED period; fall back to the txn span only when the statement doesn't carry one.**
  - **(iii) "TRANSACTIONS MATCHED: 0" ON A COMPLETE 21-TRANSACTION AUTO-PATH MONTH.** Literally true — the auto path books rather than "matches", so the matcher's counter is zero — and it **reads as failure** on a month that went perfectly. Same true-but-reads-false class as the C198·2b queue line and the ·3b re-upload toast. **Copy fix**; the count is not wrong, the label is.
  - **(v) THE f1 ATTESTATION SWEEP CONSERVATIVELY SKIPPED 3 JULY DUPLICATE CARDS.** `anomalySubjectPeriod` is **blind to f3's new date-pair fingerprints** (`dup:<vendor>:<cents>:<dateA>+<dateB>` — its regex looks for a trailing `:YYYY-MM`), **and** detection-time `invoice_ids` **don't resolve after a reload**, so neither the ref path nor the fingerprint path could place those three. They were **skipped, not mis-attested** — the sweep failed safe. **Fix: parse full dates out of the fingerprint as a second fallback.** *(Note the shape: ·3b(f3) re-keyed fingerprints and ·3b(f1) consumed them, in the same commit, and the two halves disagreed about the format. Same-commit coupling is exactly where a contract goes unchecked.)*

  - **★ STATUS: SIGNED 2026-08-16 by Alex (CPA) — this IS the Tier 1 commit spec.** Line 92 reads `- [x]`, verified on disk before it was recorded here; the diff against the tracked copy was **exactly one character**, so nothing else in the document moved. - **★★ O87 → C198·3c — SHIPPED, MERGED & LIVE (2026-08-11). All five findings closed; the batch grew two review-driven fixes, a corrected backfill and a workflow rule.** Merged to `main`, HEAD **`1f82dd9`** — three commits: **`d9b8638`** (the batch) · **`390ffae`** (D1+D3) · **`1f82dd9`** (the review bounce). **1,810 tests / 98 files green · `npm run build` clean · MIGRATIONS UNCHANGED, still applied & verified through `061`** — the follow-up-checked-update route held exactly as decided on 2026-08-09, so no RPC change and no new migration.
  - **(i) THE GATE CAN FIRE.** `payrollImportMetadata()` (pure) is stamped onto the posted entry by a `checkedRowUpdate` **after** `post_journal_entry` returns and **before** `loadAllData`, so the reload carries it. A failed stamp is audited **and** said out loud in the outcome line — the entry is correct either way; only the norm history is lost, and the next register then honestly reports that it *couldn't find* a prior run. **`depreciation.js` was genuinely inert on the identical mechanism and got the identical stamp** (D3 also made its schedule-flag write checked, §9) — its "the GL is the real guard" comment had been describing an intention, not the code, since the RPC has always dropped the key.
  - **★ THE BLAST-RADIUS CLAIM WAS HALF RIGHT, AND THE HALF THAT WAS WRONG MATTERED.** `matchPayrollBankLine` **is** structurally inert — but it is **not** what handled the June/July Gusto lines. `markAlreadyBooked` (date|amount|direction) dedup-matched them against the register's **Cr Cash leg**, and `planStatementPipeline` partitions `already_booked` out **first**, so those lines never reached `planPayrollBankLines`. Nothing needed fixing there. *(And "auto-matched at conf 100" was almost certainly the deterministic A/P clear at **99** — no path in the repo assigns a bank line a literal 100.)* **The real hazard was the opposite of the reported one:** stamping `kind:'payroll'` WAKES every reader of that key, and `flattenJournalEntries` copies one entry's `import_metadata` onto **every leg**, so the matcher's old `?? i.amount` fallback would have offered it four magnitudes to match — and a match **SUPPRESSES** the line. A Gusto tax-remittance ACH would have been swallowed as "already booked". Hence `net` in the stamp (a deliberate deviation from the decided shape) **and** the matcher now refusing that fallback outright. **Un-inerting a reader is only safe if you also give it the field it was written to read.**
  - **★★ THE REVIEW BOUNCE — the lesson of the batch.** The report claimed the backfill shape *"provably cannot start suppressing bank lines — pinned by a test."* Review restored the `?? i.amount` fallback, ran **`tests/payroll.test.js`** (the matcher suite), and watched it stay **green**. Both sides were slightly wrong and the correction is the point: **the pin did exist and did fail under mutation — but it lived in `payrollGateRoundTrip.test.js`, where nobody trying to falsify the matcher would ever run it.** Every `registerRun()` fixture in the matcher suite carries `net`, so the refusal branch was unreachable from all of them. Fixed by pinning it **where the matcher is tested**, with the real leg magnitudes (gross 4,000 · net 3,150 · employer 306 → legs 4,000 / 306 / 3,150 / 1,156, cross-checked against the REAL `buildPayrollEntry` so the fixture can't drift), and the mutation was **run, not asserted**: fallback restored → four assertions red → source restored → green. The guard comment now **names its own falsification test** and records that check.
    - **★★ LESSON — PROOFS LIVE WHERE A FALSIFIER WOULD LOOK FOR THEM.** A guard's test is not filed by which commit created it; it is filed by **where someone doubting the guard will go**. A proof in the wrong file is indistinguishable from no proof at all — it does not fail when the thing it protects is broken *by anyone who isn't already holding the map*. Sibling to the ·3a lesson (both sides of a contract tested against the same fixture): that one was a test that couldn't fail, this one is a test that couldn't be **found**. Both read as coverage.
  - **(ii) STATED PERIOD — SHIPPED, DEPLOYED, NOT YET PROVEN.** `statementPeriod` prefers the stated period **per side**, span as fallback; `period_end` added to both bank-parse profiles (it was never asked for, which is exactly why the side July got wrong was the side nobody read). Hardened past the original ask after review: well-shaped non-dates (`2026-13-45`) and **inverted** pairs fall back to the span (the span gave `min ≤ max` for free; one stated side can break it, and an inverted period silently kills `reconciliationCoversPeriod`), and the persist path now uses the validated start — `deriveStatementOpening` took it verbatim into a `date` column, where a rejected insert took statement **and** line persistence down behind a `console.warn`.
  - **(iii)** The completed-reconciliation detail no longer scores a match run that never happened; session-matched months keep their real count, and the auto-path number is **counted** off the books for that record's **own** bank account (no all-cash-codes fallback — that would have summed across accounts and double-counted cash-to-cash transfers on the one card whose job is to be exact) or renders an em dash.
  - **(v) + (D1)** `anomalySubjectPeriod` reads full dates out of f3 fingerprint tails, deliberately **not** gated on "no refs" — the live trio *had* refs that no longer resolved, which is why the old fallback was never reached. **D1 then carried the same fix to `anomalyTouchesPeriod`**, whose only consumer is the HIGH-blocks-sign-off gate: `duplicate_payment` is severity HIGH with a date-pair key, so the same dead-refs condition made the blocker silently **under-count**. The sweep failing leaves a note open; the blocker failing signs a month off over an unresolved HIGH. One fingerprint parser now feeds both, with a test pinning that they agree — two independent parsers of the same key would have been the (v) bug with the sides swapped. `rapid:` is excluded from SUBJECT and included in TOUCHES, and the asymmetry is deliberate: its key holds the window *start*, so retiring on it closes a note early (unsafe) while blocking on it blocks a month that genuinely holds the window's first charge (safe).
- **★ O108 — MIGRATION CHAIN vs LIVE: DIAGNOSED 2026-08-17, DOWNGRADED P1 → P2, AND ONE CLAIM RETRACTED. ▶ HOLD; no fix proposed.**
  - **★★ RETRACTION — CLAIM #2 WAS FALSE, AND THE CAUSE MATTERS MORE THAN THE CLAIM.** I recorded that `src/lib/constants.js` and the live chart were different COA generations — `6300` Marketing vs Repairs, `cogs` vs `cost_of_goods_sold`, `rent_occupancy` vs `rent`, Common Stock vs Owner Equity. **It is not true. `constants.js` matches live on every one of them** (`:28` `common_stock` · `:34` `product_revenue` · `:40` `cogs` · `:48` `rent_occupancy` · `:51` Repairs `6250` · `:52` Marketing `6300`).
    - **CAUSE:** I diffed **my own broken draft of `063`** against `044`, then reported the `063` side as "the client". That draft was built from `009` plus reconstruction — so what I actually measured was **`009` vs `044`**, and I labelled it `constants.js` vs live. A false attribution stacked on the original copy error, and published as **P1**.
    - **KEPT, NOT DELETED, ON PURPOSE.** A ledger that quietly loses its own false claims cannot be trusted about its true ones. The retraction is the record: this finding was wrong for two days, it drove a diagnosis session, and the same discipline that caught the bad migration — *diff it, do not trust the file* — is what caught the bad finding, one layer up.
    - **★★ AND THE CONVERSE RULE, RECORDED 2026-08-26 AFTER IT WAS BROKEN: A RETRACTION MUST BE SCOPED AS PRECISELY AS THE CLAIM IT WITHDRAWS.** An over-broad retraction **reads as diligence while deleting a correct finding** — it wears the costume of rigour and does the opposite. **Cost already paid, on `O113`:** I claimed both rate-limit walls sit at 20 files, then retracted it on discovering that the upload bucket had not bound in the observed run. But *"upload did not bind in that run"* and *"upload would not bind if `AI_LIMIT` were raised"* are different statements, and I used the first to withdraw the second. **The original claim was a counterfactual and was TRUE; it survived only because the operator restated it independently.** Before withdrawing a claim, state what it actually asserted — observed fact, mechanism, or counterfactual — and withdraw only that. A claim killed by evidence about a different claim is a claim lost to carelessness, and the ledger is no more trustworthy for it than one padded with false ones.
  - **★★ ORIGIN ESTABLISHED 2026-08-17 — `6520`/`6530` CAME FROM A HUMAN CORRECTION, NOT MACHINE INVENTION.** The live evidence: Jan 28 Toast booked at conf **85** with reasoning arguing for **6500 Technology & Software**, and Jan 31 bank fee at conf **70**, hedging **8000 vs 7100**, flagged *needs review*. Both now sit in accounts (`6520`, `6530`) their own stored reasoning contradicts. **`persistRecode` explains it exactly**: it UPDATES `journal_entry_lines.account_id` in place (App.jsx ~1290) and creates the target account first if absent (~1263) — so the line MOVES while the entry keeps its original `ai_reasoning`. The stale reasoning is the signature of an in-place recode. Then `learnFromCorrection` stored the mapping, and from **Feb 25 onward the AI proposes `6520` directly at conf 99** — the learned-mapping stamp. **The system worked as designed at every step; the defect is that creating two permanent accounts on a client's chart left no trace anywhere.**
    - **ONE CORRECTION TO THAT READING.** The deleted lines at `6500` (0 live / 1 deleted) and `7100` (0 live / 2 deleted) are **NOT** the recoded originals. An in-place recode moves the line and leaves the source account with **zero** lines, not a deleted one — so those three deleted lines are separate voided entries, unrelated to the correction story. Worth stating because the coincidence is seductive: `7100` having zero live lines is genuinely good news (Tier 1 #7's Miscellaneous hard-fail has no live instances) and it is not evidence for the recode narrative.
    - **DECIDED 2026-08-17 — `6520`/`6530` ARE BLESSED AS CANONICAL**, roles `merchant_processing_fees` and `bank_service_charges`, into both the seed and `constants.js` (→ `068`). They describe real, recurring costs for a restaurant client and were chosen by a CPA; the defect was never the accounts, it was that nothing recorded their creation.
- **★★ C201 — THE SHADOW EXECUTOR + migration `072` (2026-08-25). Both ▶ HOLD/unwired; shadow mode is OFF.**
  - **★★ "IT CANNOT BOOK" IS A PROPERTY OF THE FILE, NOT A PROMISE ABOUT IT.** `src/lib/shadowRun.js` performs **no I/O**: no client, no `fetch`, no `logAudit`. It imports exactly five pure modules — a test asserts the import list **by exact equality**, so a sixth import fails the suite — and every booking primitive is asserted **absent by name** (`persistJournalEntry`, `persistMultiLineEntry`, `post_journal_entry`, `bookToDb`, `buildJournalEntry`, `buildAccountInsert`, `ensureAccount`, `markBillPaid`, `checkedRowUpdate`). Amendment A §0 requires shadow mode to book nothing, and *"we were careful"* is not a mechanism.
  - **★ NO PHANTOM IS STRUCTURALLY POSSIBLE.** §4.1(1) makes one phantom an automatic fail; `proposeAccount` returns `null` for STRANGER **unconditionally**, so the executor cannot emit one. Pinned by a test that runs three unknown vendors and asserts all park.
  - **★ THE DIRECTORY RESOLVES A ROLE — AND A ROLE THIS COMPANY LACKS PARKS, IT DOES NOT FALL BACK.** Reaching `useAccounts`' `DEFAULT_BY_ROLE` (O108 finding 4) would return a plausible account for a role the company does not have, and `ensureAccount` would then **materialise it** — a shadow run creating accounts on eleven companies while claiming to book nothing. `directory_role_absent_from_chart` is its own park basis.
  - **EVERY PARK IS ATTRIBUTED.** `parkBasis` counts `stranger` / `no_attested_mapping` / `directory_role_absent_from_chart` / `no_directory_mapping`. Amendment A §5 requires the report to say WHICH vendors parked and why; a park rate without reasons is a number nobody can act on.
  - **MIGRATION `072`** — durable shadow records. `proposed_account_id` **NULL means PARKED** (sentinel-free, so the FK stays real and "declined to name an account" can never be confused with "named one that does not exist"); `run_id` makes §4.1(3) run-to-run drift a `group by` rather than a memory; `excluded_reason` is a **column**, because a row that is not there cannot be counted; **no jsonb blob** — every scored field is a real column, and `O95` is the standing proof of what a jsonb write boundary does to fields it does not recognise. A CHECK enforces **SCORED xor EXCLUDED**, and **no UPDATE policy exists**: editing an observation would make run-to-run drift indistinguishable from a correction. Re-run with a new `run_id` instead.
  - **TWO DESCRIPTOR COLUMNS (operator addition).** `descriptor_display` is what a human sees; `resolver_input` is the string **actually fed to identity resolution** — the RAW half for bank lines, the vendor field for READ sources. Storing only one makes a disagreement untraceable: you cannot tell whether the resolver was wrong or whether it was handed the wrong string.
  - **OWED VERIFICATIONS, tied to their blockers rather than blocking their migrations:** `066`'s VERIFY (d) — the cross-tenant write attempted as a NON-ADMIN and watched to fail — and `069`'s behavioural check both need the non-platform-admin account **ROADMAP TIER 1 #8** owes. `066` is not blocked on it; the verification is recorded as outstanding.

  - **✅ THE O87 DEBT IS SETTLED — the payroll gate fired, and its stamp landed.** Both registers auto-posted with `import_metadata` present: `kind` / `gross` / `net` / `pay_date` / `register_import_id`. **This is the check unit tests structurally could not make**, and it is exactly how ·3a shipped inert for a release.   - **★★★ AND THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST — SPECIMEN 3 REPRODUCED, EIGHT MONTHS LATER, AT THE SAME CONFIDENCE.** `ALAMO FIRE & SAFETY LLC` — **never seen, no history, no census, no directory entry** — **auto-booked $425 to 6250 Repairs & Maintenance at confidence 88**, reasoning *"fire suppression inspections… facility compliance/maintenance costs."* No human was asked. **That is Culinary Edge again**: same shape, same score, same silent booking, eight months after the incident that founded the familiarity axis. Meanwhile **Bluebonnet — attested eight months running — excepted at ~78 for the eighth time.** **THE SYSTEM TRUSTS A STRANGER MORE THAN A VENDOR IT HAS BEEN TAUGHT EIGHT TIMES, AND THE TWO NUMBERS ARE 88 AND 78.** Recorded as **the C203 justification**: this is not an argument that the ladder would be nicer, it is a demonstration that the current authority is inverted, on live data, twice, with the numbers written down.
  - **★ P0-1 — THE RATE LIMIT BLOCKS ANY PAYING CLIENT. DESIGNATED `O113`.** 21 of 36 invoices died at HTTP 429. **The throttle is OURS** (`AI_LIMIT = 60`/user/hour, `ai-proxy/index.ts:17`, via `bump_rate_limit`, migration `021`), not upstream. **Three calls per file confirmed** — `classify-document` → `extract-invoices-batch` → `code-invoices-batch` — and **the "batch" profiles batch WITHIN a document, not ACROSS files**, while the queue processes one file at a time. The observed break at ~15 files implies a **fourth call the structural read does not account for; not pinned, and recorded as unpinned rather than guessed at.** A real restaurant is 40–80 invoices/month and onboarding wants 2–3 months, so **a new client's first action exceeds the limit by 3–10×.**
  - **★ P0-2 — THE DUPLICATE DETECTOR IS DETECTING THE INVOICE-TO-PAYMENT LIFECYCLE. DESIGNATED `O114`.** `findDuplicate` keys on normalised vendor + amount within 1% in a date window — and an invoice and the bank line paying it are **by construction** the same vendor and amount days apart. **The detector is right that they are one economic event and wrong about which kind.** The bank line already booked the expense; the invoice books it again, so **only the card stands between this and a double-counted P&L — and the natural answer, "New charge — book it", is the one that corrupts the books.** Wrong-by-default on the most common shape in small-business accounting. Bluebonnet ×4 flagged against last month's identical $145 delivery: same-vendor-same-amount is the NORMAL case for a weekly fixed-fee vendor. **P2 is downstream of this:** the deliberate Hill Country ±18 transposition was never tested because **there is no invoice-to-payment matching to mismatch on.**
  - **★ P1-1 — THE SUMMARY CONTRADICTS THE BOOKED ACCOUNT. DESIGNATED `O115`.** Queue says Lone Star *"as a client meal"*; ledger says 5000 COGS. `plainCategoryPhrase` (`clarify.js:165`) **re-derives a category from the entry's text** rather than reading the account already on the row — an independent guess about an answer sitting three feet away. Q2 world-claim, in the owner's seat.
