  - **★ THE SECURITY LADDER (tracked, IN ORDER — each rung gates a growth stage). ★ ALL SIX ANCHORED 2026-08-11** — the sweep found every rung carrying **no roadmap row at all**: six live gates parked inside Tier 1 #11, an item marked ✅ DONE. **s1 → ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #11a · s2 → #11b · s3 → #11c · s4 → TIER 2 (probe-as-script) · s5 → §2 Security `O100` · s6 → §2 Security `O101`.** Note what s2 holds: checks **(b)** signed/expiring document URLs and **(c)** key hygiene from the #11 audit, never separately reported as verified — so #11 was marked DONE with two of its four checks living on as an unanchored rung. The audit proved the database boundary. These are the rungs above it; **each is a gate, not a wish**, and the gate names the stage it blocks.
    - **(s1) 2FA sweep on operator accounts — Supabase, GitHub, Vercel, the app itself. GATE: THIS WEEK.** First for a reason: `is_platform_admin()` makes **operator credentials the master key to every tenant** (§3 Option A). Every wall the audit just verified is downstream of one password. The strongest RLS in the world is a rounding error next to a phished operator login.
    - **(s2) Service-key grep + document-URL check** — verify **no service-role key ships in the client bundle** (grep `dist/`, not just `src/`), and confirm document URLs are **signed and expiring**, never permanent. **GATE: before the warm channel widens.**
    - **(s3) AI-vendor data path IN WRITING** — which extraction provider sees statement contents, **retention terms**, **training-use terms**. **GATE: before the warm channel widens.** This is the **one gap in the client-facing story** below until it closes.
    - **(s4) Probe-as-script** — the cross-tenant probe becomes an **automated test in the suite**, not a memory of an afternoon. **GATE: next code session touching tests.** *(The C195(7) lesson applied to security: assert the mechanism FIRES. A probe nobody re-runs and a wall that quietly fell look identical from the outside.)*
    - **(s5) External fixed-price RLS / security review** — a second set of eyes on our own audit. **GATE: before the warm channel widens past the first operators.** Stated plainly: **we reviewed a thing we built**, and that limitation doesn't go away because the result was good.
    - **(s6) Third-party penetration test. GATE: before strangers at scale.** **SOC 2 remains a company milestone, unscheduled** (a program with an auditor and a calendar; nothing above depends on it).

  - **★ THE CLIENT-FACING STORY IS NOW WRITABLE — from verified facts ONLY (Tier 2, unblocked 2026-08-09).** What may be said today, because each clause has evidence behind it: **database-layer tenant isolation verified table-by-table (33/33) and probed live with a non-privileged account**; **documents held in private, company-scoped, encrypted storage**; **ledger writes role-gated**; and — the most credible claim of the four — **found-and-fixed, with the evidence attached (`061`)**. A security story that admits a real finding and shows the fix reads as true; one that claims perfection reads as marketing. **HARD RULE: the claims must never exceed that verified list.** The AI-vendor data path (s3) is **the single open gap** — until its answer is in writing, the story must **stay silent on what the extraction provider retains**, and must not imply otherwise by omission.

- **★★ TIER 1 #8 — TEAM INVITES COULD NEVER BE ACCEPTED (fixed 2026-08-29, C225; migration `076` ▶ HOLD).** The whole flow was built — table, token, signup banner, `accept_invite` — and **the last step could not complete.** `company_users_role_check` allows exactly `owner | admin | accountant | viewer`; the invite form offered **"Member"**, which is in none of them, and `accept_invite` inserts the invite's role straight into `company_users`. **An invite created with the DEFAULT role hit the check constraint the moment the person clicked the link.**
  - **★★ AND THE SAME MISMATCH DISABLED THE READ-ONLY ROLE.** `isMember = userRole === "member"` was **permanently false**, so the delete/void controls, the AI's mutating actions and the settings sub-tabs were gated on a condition that could never be true. The DB still refused a viewer's writes (RLS role gates, C199) — so nothing was corrupted, and what a viewer got was a screen of buttons that would fail: the "refusing on click" anti-pattern arrived at by accident. **The app's "member" and the database's "viewer" were one idea under two names, and only one existed.**
  - **★ THE GUARD PARSES `company_users_role_check` OUT OF THE BASELINE SCHEMA rather than restating the list.** A copy of the allowed roles in the test would have been a FOURTH place for the vocabulary to drift, which is the entire shape of this bug.
  - **▶ `076` (HELD) fixes what only the database can:** the column default, any pending invite already carrying a rejected role, and **the check constraint that would have made this impossible** — `company_invites.role` was free text, so two tables described one concept and only one enforced it. Its VERIFY (c) makes the constraint actually REFUSE something in a rolled-back transaction.
  - **★ This also unblocks the item below**: creating a non-platform-admin account is now possible from the app.
- **Backlog — sign-off DB-layer gating needs a non-platform-admin acceptance test (fold into team-invites; not built). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #8.** *(O87 note: the s4 probe-as-script rung now needs the same non-platform-admin account, so the two share a prerequisite.)* O83 role-gating verification flipped the operator's Franklin Ave role `admin → viewer` (DB CHECK rejects `'member'`; `'viewer'` is the valid non-privileged role) and confirmed **live** that the **UI layer** correctly blocks a non-reviewer from sign-off (`ReviewView` `canSignOff = isReviewer = canAttestPeriod('viewer') = false` → no sign-off card/button/month-picker/override). BUT the **DB-layer** rejection could **not** be proven with the operator's account: `is_company_reviewer(cid)` (migration 051) is `is_platform_admin() OR role IN ('admin','accountant')`, and the operator's email is the platform admin — so the policy would have **permitted** the write regardless of company role. Full end-to-end DB-layer proof (that `period_signoffs` INSERT is rejected for a non-reviewer) requires a **non-platform-admin test user**, which needs team invites. Fold in as an acceptance test when invites ship. *(Related finding surfaced same session: the app's `isMember = role === "member"` can never be true — the `company_users` role CHECK allows only `owner/admin/accountant/viewer`, never `'member'`; the app's "member" concept and the DB's `'viewer'` are out of sync. Reconcile the naming as part of the roles/invites work.)*

- **Backlog — Trust panel "Documents" queue line reads as an unearned/pending check (not built; UX-honesty finding). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 "Activity-feed jargon scrub + trust-panel Documents queue glyph" — shares that line with the Home Activity feed item below.** The owner `TrustPanel` (`src/components/views/TrustPanel.jsx`) mixes two line semantics on one checklist: **Reviewed** and **Nothing wrong** are EARNED states (green `✓` only when verified — the O90 no-vacuous-pass rule), while **Documents** (`lines.captured`, sourced from `ownerTrust.js`) is a QUEUE — when empty it renders the `info`/neutral state whose glyph is a hollow gray dot `◦` (see the `Line` component: `glyph = state === "ok" ? "✓" : state === "attention" ? "•" : "◦"`). The **semantics are correct** — an empty queue must NOT get a celebratory green check (that would be exactly the vacuous-pass class we've been eliminating) — but **visually**, a gray dot sitting next to green checks reads as "pending/unfinished," and the O83 operator himself found it confusing. **Target:** differentiate QUEUE lines from STATE lines visually so "empty queue" doesn't resemble "unearned check" — e.g. a subtle inbox/tray icon instead of the dot, or restructure Documents to render as a **count chip** ("0 waiting") rather than a checklist row. When documents ARE waiting it should **escalate visually** (amber + count). **Keep the earned-state lines (Reviewed / Nothing wrong) exactly as they are** — do not touch the green-only-when-verified gate; this is a presentation change to the queue line only.

- **Backlog — Vendors tab: system/deposit entries leak in as vendors + blanket 1099 tagging is wrong (O83 finding, CPA-liability class). DESIGNATED: the LEAK → ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 ("vendors-tab leak: `vendorSummary` needs a source/side filter" — flagged demo-lethal); the 1099 half → TIER 1 #10 (minimal default-OFF) and TIER 3 (full derived eligibility).** Two defects on the Vendors surface (`VendorsView.jsx`, fed by `vendorSummary` in `App.jsx` + the `contacts` table). **(1) Non-vendor rows leak into the vendor list.** `vendorSummary` (`App.jsx` ~5586) keys a map on `inv.vendor` over ALL flattened ledger rows with **no filter on `source` or entry side** — so the system **opening-balance** entry shows up as a vendor named "Opening balances as of 2026-01-01" (Edit button and all), and a **revenue-deposit** line spawned a zero-transaction "Toast POS" vendor shell (deposits correctly aren't vendor payments, yet the description created a vendor). Some also arrive as real `contacts` rows via `createOrUpdateContact` in the booking path. **Target:** system/source entries (`opening_balance`, adjustments, and any non-A/P leg) must NEVER derive or create a vendor — filter `vendorSummary` to expense/A-P-side rows with a real counterparty, exclude system `source`s, and stop customer-side/deposit lines from spawning contacts; retro-filter the ones already created. **(2) Blanket "+1099" badge is wrong and creates CPA filing liability.** The badge renders off `v.is1099` for `fromContact` vendors (`VendorsView.jsx:49,233`) and is effectively defaulted-on rather than derived — on Franklin Ave nearly every vendor carried it, including **goods** vendors (Roma, Lone Star, Hill Country, Kitchen Supply — food/equipment purchases are **not** 1099-NEC reportable) and a utility. Only rent (Franklin Ave Properties, if non-corp) plausibly qualifies. Wrong flags → **wrong 1099 filings under the CPA's name at year-end.** **Target:** 1099 eligibility must be **derived** (services vs goods by GL category/`system_role`, payee **entity type** (corps exempt), and the **$600 `IRS_1099_THRESHOLD`**), or at absolute minimum **default-OFF with explicit CPA opt-in per vendor**. **Pairs with the Taxes/1099s Admin flow** whenever that gets built. *(Amounts on the tab verified correct — Lone Star $2,339.33 = both invoices; totals matched the answer key. This is a classification/listing defect, not a math defect.)*

- **Backlog — Document library misses bank statements + weak metadata (O83 finding, sign-off-trust class). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 doc-library line, now widened to (a) route ALL intake paths through `storeDocument` [bank path done in C185] + (b) derived type + (c) document date.** Three defects on the Documents library (`storeDocument` in `App.jsx:530`; `docsFilterType`; `DocumentPreviewModal`/docs view). **(a) Not every intake path stores its file.** `storeDocument` is called ONLY from the universal-upload path (`App.jsx:3691` invoice, `3947` contract); **`handleBankFile` (Bank Import) and the Reconcile flow parse the statement but never call `storeDocument`.** So Franklin Ave's January bank statement — uploaded **4×** across Bank Import + Reconcile — is **absent from the library**, despite the header promising bank statements are stored. This is also why the 20 bank-line entries render **"No source document attached"**: their source PDF was never retained. **For the sign-off trust story this is load-bearing — the primary document behind a reconciled, attested period must exist in the system.** **Target:** route EVERY intake path (bank import, reconcile, payroll, QBO — not just universal upload) through `storeDocument`, linked to the entries/reconciliation it produced. *(Ties to the §11 North Star + `bank_account_id`-linkage backlog: persisting the parsed statement is the same missing-linkage workstream.)* **(b) Type is defaulted, not derived.** The universal path hardcodes `storeDocument(..., "invoice", ...)` (`App.jsx:3691`), so a utility bill and a register receipt both file as **"Invoice"**, and the filter chips imply an invoice-only taxonomy. **Target:** derive `document_type` from the same AI extraction that already classifies the doc (invoice / bill / receipt / bank statement / contract …) and broaden the filter chips. **(c) Only the upload date is first-class.** Cards show `uploaded_at` (e.g. "Jul 20") but not the document's OWN date, which we **already parse** during extraction. **Target:** capture and surface the document date as first-class metadata (sort/filter by it) so the library is usable for finding "the January statement" rather than "the thing I uploaded on the 20th."

- **Backlog — pattern-level duplicate suppression for legitimate recurring same-amount vendors (predicted at C178 review, confirmed live O83 Feb; not built).** C181 tightened the duplicate_payment window to 7 days so biweekly payroll / monthly insurance no longer false-fire. But a genuinely **weekly** same-amount vendor (e.g. Bluebonnet Linen at 7-day spacing) still trips the window every week, and each month's new charges carry **new fingerprints**, so the "duplicate" flags **re-fire monthly** and never converge — the same recurring vendor keeps generating HIGH anomalies the CPA must dismiss again and again. The real fix is **pattern-level suppression**: recognize a vendor as legitimately recurring at a cadence (the `recurring_transactions` machinery + `detectRecurringPatterns` in `insights.js` already model this) and **exclude its on-cadence same-amount charges from duplicate detection entirely** (a charge that matches the vendor's known recurring amount+cadence is expected, not a double-pay). This belongs with the recurring-transactions machinery, not the duplicate detector's date window. Until then, weekly-service duplicates are dismissable but noisy.

- **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (C259) — the opening-balance discrepancy is a durable note.** It lived in React state alone, so a reload or a navigation lost it — **the O97 in-memory class, on a finding that says the client's STARTING POSITION may be wrong.**
  - **★★★ THE DEFERRAL REASON WAS REAL AND IS NOW CONFIRMED IN CODE: `reconcileAnomalies` AUTO-RESOLVES ANY OPEN ROW THE DETECTOR DID NOT JUST EMIT** (`anomalies.js:216`). Correct for everything the pure detector owns — the condition left the ledger, so the note should go — and **fatal here**, because this needs the STATEMENT's stated opening, a number **not in the ledger** and never re-derivable by re-running over it. Inserted without an exemption the note would appear and resolve a second later, forever: **a feature that flickers is indistinguishable from one that was never built.**
  - **`IMPERATIVE_ANOMALY_TYPES` IS THE UNBLOCKING MOVE, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY ONE ENTRY LONG.** A type listed there is never auto-resolved, **so it must carry its own resolve condition** — adding one without a way out turns *"it vanishes on reload"* into *"it can never be cleared"*, the opposite failure and just as bad. A test pins the set's exact contents, and another pins that a DETECTOR-owned note still auto-resolves: **an exemption that swallowed everything would leave the queue meaning nothing.**
  - **★★ KEYED ON THE ACCOUNT AND PERIOD, NEVER THE AMOUNT.** Re-uploading the same statement must not stack notes, and **the difference CHANGING is not a new problem — it is the same problem, still there.**
  - **★★★ MEDIUM, NOT HIGH, AND THAT IS A DECISION.** HIGH blocks sign-off, and **C179 established that a difference the outstanding-items chain fully explains is not an alarm** — frightening a client over a $275 uncleared cheque is the exact false alarm this product keeps removing. Worth a reviewer's attention; not a block.
  - **A COMPLETED RECONCILIATION SETTLES IT — at or before the period it covered, never after.** A later period's opening is a question that reconciliation did not answer, and resolving it would clear a note **nobody has looked at.** *(orig: deferred from C179; O83 follow-up to C178.)* C179 fixed the opening-discrepancy comparand (GL cash at period start, not the opening row). Wiring the discrepancy as a **persisted anomaly** (C178 lifecycle) was deferred because it doesn't fit the `runAnomalyDetection` model: that reconcile AUTO-RESOLVES by re-running a pure function over the **ledger**, but an opening discrepancy needs the **statement's stated opening** (not in the ledger), so it can't be re-derived on a later scan. Wiring it would require a **bespoke imperative insert** at detection time (in `handleBankFile`) with its own fingerprint + a **custom auto-resolve** condition (resolves when the opening is corrected / the reconciliation is completed, not when a ledger scan stops seeing it). Today it surfaces as `openingDiscrepancyFlag` (React state) + an audit row + a notification — durable enough, just not in the `anomalies` table. **Target:** if opening discrepancies should live in the unified anomaly queue/trust-gate, add a non-detection anomaly source (imperative insert + explicit resolve on opening-correction) — a small dedicated lifecycle, not a `runAnomalyDetection` type.

- **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (C239, migration `080`) — anomaly reviewer-comment feature.** *(Kept for the reasoning, which is the durable part.)* The `anomaly_comments` table is append-only by design — SELECT and INSERT policies and nothing else — because **a comment that can be edited afterwards is not evidence of anything.** Dismissal stayed reviewer-only and `080` moved that gate into the DATABASE, where `056` had left it as a UI convention. *(orig: not built; O83 follow-up to C178.)* C178 made anomalies persisted records with dismissal **permanently reviewer-gated** (dismissing judges a condition acceptable — a review act; client-side dismissal would be self-attestation one anomaly at a time). A client may still legitimately want to give the reviewer **context** on a flagged item ("that Sysco double-charge was a deposit + a bill, not a duplicate"). **Target:** a lightweight **comment/note** thread on an `anomalies` row (owner may ADD context; only the reviewer DISMISSES) — a comment is not a clear action and never changes `status`. This preserves separation of duties (the owner-facing panel already shows the honest view; see `ownerTrust.js` "your accountant is taking a look") while letting the client inform the judgment. Small: a `anomaly_comments` child table (or an `entity_refs`-style jsonb thread) + owner-visible input in the trust panel + reviewer-visible display in the Review Unusual-Activity section. Do NOT add a client dismiss verb.

- **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-29 — Review-queue anomaly cards now show the transactions inline.** A linked entry that no longer resolves is REPORTED rather than silently dropped (the O87(v) lesson). *(orig: O83 Feb; the asymmetry was that the OWNER's panel linked to the entries and the REVIEWER — the person actually making the call — had the least context.)* Asymmetry: the OWNER's home trust panel gives each anomaly **"View transaction →"** and **"Review →"** links (`DashboardView.jsx`), but the CPA **Review** screen — where dismiss-with-reason actually happens (the reviewer-only judgment, C178) — offers **only Dismiss**. So the reviewer must judge a possible duplicate/recurring charge **without seeing the underlying entries** — exactly backwards (the person making the call has the least context). **Target:** the anomaly cards in the Review Unusual-Activity section (`ReviewView.jsx`) should link to the involved transactions — **`entity_refs` are already on the row** (the linked journal-entry ids), so it's a lookup + navigate, mirroring the owner panel's `openTxn`. **Ideally show the pair INLINE** (the two entries' dates + amounts side by side) so the duplicate/recurring judgment is one glance rather than a navigate-away. Pairs naturally with the reviewer-comment feature above (both touch the same Review anomaly card). Small; do it the next time the anomaly queue is edited.

- **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (C239, migration `080`) — evidence-attached anomaly resolution.** Optional by design and pinned by a test asserting the prompt function **cannot express a refusal**: requiring support would produce attachments chosen for being nearest, **which is worse than none because it LOOKS like support.** The soft prompt reads the linked entries' AMOUNTS, never the anomaly's words. *(orig: operator idea, O83 Feb.)* Extend the reviewer's dismiss flow so a dismissal MAY carry **supporting document(s)** — a vendor agreement, the second (distinct) invoice, correspondence — attached as proof the flagged charge is legitimate. Store via the **document library** (`storeDocument`) and link from the `anomalies` row (`evidence_doc_ids` jsonb, mirroring `entity_refs`), surfaced in the dismissal history so a future auditor sees *why* it was cleared, not just that it was. **Reason stays REQUIRED (floor); evidence is OPTIONAL but first-class (ceiling)** — never hard-require support: legitimate dismissals often have none (verbal confirmations, obvious re-reads), and forcing an upload would push reviewers to attach junk. Consider a **soft prompt** (suggest, don't block) to attach evidence above a **$ threshold**, where an unsupported clear is higher-risk. **Related auto-resolve mechanic:** an uploaded doc that BOOKS and gives the flagged charges **distinct backing** (e.g. two same-amount charges turn out to be two separate invoices) dissolves the anomaly's *condition* → the C178 reconcile **auto-resolves** it on the next scan, no dismissal needed — the cleanest outcome, and it flows through the existing lifecycle for free. **Pairs with** the owner-comment feature (client uploads the support, reviewer decides) and the Review-card inspection-affordances item above — all three touch the same Review anomaly card and the same `anomalies`-row-plus-jsonb-links shape, so build them together.

- **✅ SHIPPED (C198·3b(e), `30cf680`) — burn/runway revenue-blind, false runway alarm for profitable companies.** *(Was ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #9, folded into C198; a profitable month now reads the same figure as COVERAGE and carries no alarm. Original finding kept below for the record.)* *(orig: not built; honesty-violation class).** `businessHealth` (`reports.js`) computes "Monthly burn" via `computeBurnRate`/`burnRateDetail`, which is the trailing-3-month average of **GROSS EXPENSES only** (`plLegs(i, glIsExpense)`), ignoring revenue entirely; it then feeds that into `computeRunway(cash, burn)`. O83: Franklin Ave — a **profitable** January (+$3,174.33 net income, cash grew) — displayed "Monthly burn $13,142.15 · Runway ~1 mo · **Needs attention**" and self-contradicting copy "You're profitable **with ~1 month of runway**." A false "you're almost out of money" is the **same honesty violation as the false-greens we've been eliminating all week — in the scary direction** (arguably worse: it frightens a healthy client). **Target:** burn = **net cash change** (or net income as a proxy), period-normalized correctly. A profitable / cash-positive run rate → **no runway warning at all**, and a positive frame ("You added $3,174 in January"); runway warnings reserved for genuinely **cash-negative** run rates, in plain language. **Blast-radius caveat:** `computeBurnRate` is the SINGLE shared burn figure across the dashboard card, the runway drill, and the AI financial snapshot (deliberately, so they never drift). Redefining "burn" as net-of-revenue must flow through all three consistently — don't fork a second definition — and re-check `businessHealth`'s `concerns`/`tone` gating (the `runway < 6` / `burnUpPct` branches) and the KPI strip's Burn Multiple, which already handles the profitable case correctly (`netBurn <= 0 → "0.0× (profitable)"`) and is the model to mirror.

- **Backlog — reconcile should not re-upload an already-parsed statement (bank-linkage workstream; ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 "ReconView sources persisted statement lines instead of re-parsing" — C185 made this possible).** The Reconcile tab makes the user re-upload + re-parse the SAME statement Bank Import already read — wasteful and fragile (it's how the O83 BUG-1 parse regression surfaced: two independent parse call-sites). Root: imported statement lines aren't persisted anywhere queryable — there's no `bank_account_id` linkage on booked entries and no stored statement-line table, so the reconcile flow can't source them. Target: persist parsed statement lines against a `bank_account_id` (+ statement period/balance) at import, and have Reconcile source them from storage instead of re-parsing. This is the **third symptom of the missing bank-account linkage** — after (1) the decoupled Settings `current_balance` (booked statement txns don't update it) and (2) the placeholder-detection residual (a real account looks like the seed) — all resolved cleanly once entries carry a `bank_account_id`. See O83 Issue 3 (deferred).

- **✅ SHIPPED — Reconcile's Bank Ending Balance prefills from the persisted statement.** *(Verified 2026-08-29 as already true end-to-end and pinned as a chain test — the item had gone stale: the prefill and the exception-card link were built separately and nobody had followed the two together.)* *(orig: O83 Feb; North Star adjacent, cheap interim.)* PROMOTED onto C198 by O86 finding (l) — and now sourced from the PERSISTED statement (`bank_statements.stated_ending_balance`, C185) rather than a re-parse.** The parse **already extracts `endingBalance`** (the opening-balance adoption flow uses it — `deriveStatementOpening` / the proposal path), but the reconcile completion bar leaves the **bank ending balance field blank** and makes the user **retype a number the app just read**. O83 live-drive: the operator **stalled here expecting it to be automatic**, and manual retyping invites **transposition errors** against the verified-balance guard (`statementBalanceVerified` / `canCompleteReconciliation`) — a fat-fingered ending balance either false-blocks a clean reconciliation or (worse) "verifies" a wrong number. **Fix:** prefill the field from the parsed `endingBalance`, clearly **editable** and labeled **"from your statement"** — the user **confirms rather than transcribes**. Keep the verified-balance guard as-is (a confirmed prefill is still a real, verified balance). **Full automation** of this step (statement-derived ending balance → auto-completed verified reconciliation, no field at all) belongs to the **★ North Star pipeline**; the prefill is the cheap interim that removes the stall + the transcription-error class now. Lives in `ReconView.jsx` (the completion bar's `statementBalance` input) — source the value the same way the opening-proposal flow already does.

- **Backlog — business-type-driven chart of accounts (HIGH, pre-launch; not built). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #6.** `CompanySetup` asks for business type but `seed_company_accounts` seeds an **identical generic SaaS-flavored COA** regardless. O83: Franklin Ave Pizza (type Restaurant) got Subscription Revenue, Technology & Software (SaaS), ROU Asset Amortization, ASC 842 lease accounts — and **lacked** Merchant/Processing Fees, Bank Service Charges, and any food-cost structure. This causes real miscategorization *because the AI categorizer is constrained to the chart it's given*: the Toast merchant fee (bank memo literally "MERCHANT FEES JAN") booked to **6500 SaaS** because no correct account existed, and the bank service fee oscillated **8000 Interest ↔ 7100 Miscellaneous** across runs for the same reason. **Target:** per-business-type COA templates chosen at company creation (drive off the business type already collected). The **restaurant** template must include COGS-Food, Merchant/Processing Fees, Bank Charges and OMIT SaaS/lease-accounting accounts. Industry-appropriate seeding removes a whole class of miscategorization at the root (upstream of the categorizer, the clarification floor, and the control totals). Lives in `seed_company_accounts` (RPC) + `DEFAULT_CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS`/role map (`src/lib/constants.js`); keep the `system_role` scheme (§4) so downstream role lookups still resolve per template.

- **Backlog — onboarding vendor teach-in → ENTITY PROFILES (ROADMAP §0 TIER 3; sequenced AFTER business-type COA seeding (Tier 1 #6); supercharged by QBO import).** New-client cold start currently relies on **reactive** learning (a human recodes → a vendor→account mapping is written, `learnFromCorrection`/`clientProfile.js`), so the FIRST real month books against cold guesses and only gets smarter after mistakes. **Front-load it instead:** at onboarding, ingest **2–3 months of historical bank statements + any invoice pile** (or a **QBO export — the richest source**, years of confirmed vendor→account history), derive the **vendor universe** (name, typical amounts, cadence, and a proposed account mapping against the **business-type COA** above), and present the reviewing CPA a **single bulk confirmation table**. Each confirmation writes a **`human_correction`-grade vendor mapping BEFORE the first live month runs**, so the pipeline's first real statement books against **confirmed priors** instead of cold guesses (turns C186's cold start warm). **Design constraint:** teaching must be **derived from artifacts the client already has — never homework** (no "upload an example invoice per vendor"). **CPA-as-teacher is the intended onboarding motion** (fits the CPA referral channel — the reviewer confirms the mappings once, up front). **Future extension:** per-vendor **document exemplars** for parse robustness — only if parse accuracy proves to need it. Pairs with the business-type COA (the mapping target) and QBO import (the richest teach-in source). **Generalization (same architecture, later phases):** vendor teach-in is the FIRST instance of **ENTITY PROFILES** — per-client priors on any recurring entity (**vendors, customers, payroll, rent/loan obligations**), each holding **document exemplars**, **typical money-movement patterns** (amount, cadence, channel), and **confirmed accounting treatment**. **Customers are the second target:** A/R deposit matching (mangled descriptors, batch deposits, short-pays) benefits most from knowing how each customer typically pays. Teach-in **populates** profiles from historical artifacts at onboarding; the live learning layer **updates** them from corrections thereafter. **Build as ONE profile system, not per-type features.**

- **Backlog — AI confidence calibration + reasoning-consistency guard (not built; ties the clarification-flow work + Cardinal Principle). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 #7.** O83: the Lone Star bill booked to **6400 Travel & Entertainment at 92% confidence** while its OWN stored reasoning argued the items are "direct product costs properly classified as **Cost of Goods Sold**" — an internally contradictory rationale that sailed past the 70% clarification floor. **Cheap guard:** when the reasoning text's recommended account disagrees with the *booked* account, CAP confidence below the clarification floor (`AI_CONFIDENCE_ASK_FLOOR`) so it routes to the plain-language question flow instead of auto-booking (a self-contradiction is exactly a "needs a human" signal — cf. §5 action sandbox, `confidenceFlag.js`/`autoBookDecision`). Two smaller finds bundled here: **(a)** owner-visible AI **reasoning leaks jargon** ("50% deductible under current tax law", raw GL codes) — reasoning *shown to owners* needs the same Cardinal-Principle scrub as clarification copy (`containsOwnerJargon`), while the CPA-facing `reasoning` field may stay technical (§9 exemptions); **(b)** the payment-side entry renders the literal placeholder **"Loaded from database"** in its AI Reasoning panel (from `flattenJournalEntries`' default `reasoning`) — a display bug: suppress/replace the placeholder for entries that have no real AI rationale (payments, clearings, opening balances).

- **Backlog — Home Activity feed renders GL jargon on the owner surface (Cardinal Principle; O83). DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 2 "Activity-feed jargon scrub + trust-panel Documents queue glyph" — paired with the trust-panel item above. (Distinct from Tier 1 #7's owner-jargon scrub, which covers AI reasoning copy, not audit detail rendered into the feed.)** The Home "Activity" feed (`DashboardView.jsx:788`) renders `auditLog` rows verbatim as `a.detail || a.action` — and `markBillPaid` (`App.jsx:5283-5285`) bakes GL notation into that detail: *"paid Franklin Ave Properties LP · $2400.00 via ACH / Bank Transfer · **GL Dr AP/Cr Cash posted**."* Everything through "Bank Transfer" is good plain language; the `· GL Dr AP/Cr Cash posted` tail (from `glStr`, and the AR twin `Dr Cash/Cr AR`) is bookkeeping jargon that belongs in the **audit trail, not the client feed** — a Cardinal-Principle violation on an owner surface (same class as the owner-jargon find above). **Target:** keep the GL notation in `audit_log` (ideally in the structured `after_state`, where the `payment_entry_id` already lives, or a separate technical field — NOT the human `detail` the feed shows), and render the owner Activity line without it. Cleanest is to **stop appending `glStr` to the `logAudit` detail string** and instead scrub owner-facing activity lines (a `containsOwnerJargon`-style strip on the feed), so the audit detail stays complete for the CPA/audit view while the client sees only plain language. Small; audit-trail completeness must not regress. *(Broader: the Activity feed rendering raw audit detail is a general leak vector — any audit detail with GL/debit-credit wording will surface on Home; a feed-level scrub covers the class, not just this one line.)*

- **★★ COLD START — BOTH HALVES SHIPPED 2026-08-29 (C223 templates, C224 the hard-fail guard).** The joint acceptance test is met by construction now: a business-type template gives a restaurant somewhere correct to put CO2 tanks, and `autoBookDecision` refuses to auto-book into `7100`/`7150` when it can NAME the vendor.
  - **★★ THE SEED RPC WAS NOT TOUCHED, AND §6 IS WHY.** Five definitions of `seed_company_accounts` in the repo, the highest never applied and unappliable, so a `create or replace` must start from `pg_get_functiondef` against live. The templates are a CLIENT-SIDE OVERLAY on the generic seed — no migration, no RPC change, nothing to get wrong about a function nobody can read.
  - **★ AND THE ITEM'S PREMISE WAS WRONG, WHICH MADE THAT THE NATURAL SHAPE ANYWAY: `CompanySetup` DOES NOT ASK FOR BUSINESS TYPE.** It asks for a name; the type arrives a step later in the onboarding profile. So the chart is necessarily seeded before the type is known, and applying the overlay when we learn it is the only correct placement rather than a workaround.
  - **★ ADDITIVE. Never a delete, never an account that carries transactions, never a code the company already has** (a rename is theirs). `Other` is deliberately empty — a business we cannot name is one whose chart we should not be opinionated about.
  - **★★ THE O108 GUARD FIRED THE MOMENT THE NEW DOOR OPENED, WHICH IS ITS ENTIRE JOB — and the right response was to WIDEN IT, NOT TO MISLABEL THE EVENT.** `account_materialized` means "the system invented an account mid-flight"; a business-type template is a deliberate batched setup. Emitting that action to satisfy the test would have made the audit log claim the system invented seven accounts on its own. The accepted actions are now a NAMED SET, the way `NON_ATTESTING_EXCEPTIONS` is.
  - **★★ `Alamo Ice` SLIPPED PAST THE CONFIDENCE FLOOR BECAUSE THE MODEL WAS CONFIDENT ABOUT THE WRONG THING — it was sure it did not know.** A confident booking into a bucket that MEANS uncertainty is a contradiction in terms, and no confidence threshold can catch it, which is why the guard reads the ACCOUNT rather than the score.
  - **★ A MUTATION THAT DIDN'T BITE CAUGHT AN UNVERIFIED CLAIM IN MY OWN COMMENT.** The source said the check's placement before the materiality flag was load-bearing; the fixture sat at 92%, where no flag condition fires, so the ordering could not matter for it. Re-pinned at 85% on $6,000, where both rules genuinely apply and only the REASON differs — and the reason is what the card says.
- **★ Cold-start categorization is the top-of-funnel first impression. DESIGNATED: the joint acceptance test for ROADMAP §0 TIER 1 **#6** (business-type COA, ✅ shipped C223) and **#7** (confidence calibration, open); the demo-company half is ROADMAP §0 launch-week "a demo company with data already in it".** *(Anchored 2026-08-30 by the orphan sweep — it was the ONE bullet in §11 carrying no id, no commit and no tier line, held only by the phrase "the two items above", which is exactly the cross-reference-not-an-anchor case the 2026-08-08 run recorded: **"is it scheduled?" must be answerable from the item itself, not by grepping for a sibling that happens to mention it.** Status at anchoring: **#6 shipped**, and C224 shipped the other half of the hard-fail — a confident booking into the "couldn't tell" bucket now always asks — so the acceptance test is **half met**, with #7's calibration the remaining half.)* In the **first live demo on an unconfigured test entity**, a clearly-identifiable **utility invoice booked to Miscellaneous in front of a prospect** — the single worst cold-start failure (a document any human reads at a glance, mis-booked, with no smart question asked). This is the intersection of the two workstreams above: the **business-type COA** (an unconfigured/generic entity may lack the right expense account, so the categorizer has nowhere correct to put it) and the **confidence/reasoning guard** (a low-confidence or account-less booking should ASK a plain-language question, never silently fall back to Miscellaneous). Together they own the promise: **"the first document a new signup uploads books correctly OR asks a smart question"** — never a silent wrong bucket. Treat that sentence as the joint acceptance test for both items; a Miscellaneous fallback on a recognizable vendor/category is a hard fail. **Separately — the DEMO experience may deserve its own pass:** a **pre-seeded showcase company** (representative COA + realistic booked history + a clean sample document) so sales demos never run on a cold-start entity at all. That's a distinct workstream from fixing cold-start itself (which still must be fixed for real first-time signups) — a demo shouldn't be the thing that surfaces cold-start gaps, and cold-start shouldn't be demoed raw.

- **★★ `rate_limit` (migration `021`) IS DEAD SCHEMA AS OF `086` — and its emptiness reads as a product defect (recorded 2026-09-02).** The rolling window must count a TRAILING sixty minutes, which an hourly aggregate cannot express, so `086` writes **one row per call to `rate_limit_events`** and never touches the old table again. **Do not drop it** — `021`'s `bump_rate_limit` survives as `074`'s rollback path, and the table is that function's storage.
  - **★★★ THE COST IS NOT THE DEAD ROWS, IT IS THAT A QUERY AGAINST IT LIES IN A CONVINCING DIRECTION.** A drive census pointed at `rate_limit` reported **"AI budget used: (none)"** beside **twenty documents refused by the AI limiter, each carrying the limiter's own message**. That is a flat contradiction, it looks exactly like a limiter charging refusals or a counter failing to persist, and it cost a diagnostic round-trip chasing a defect that did not exist. **A retired table does not report that it is retired; it reports zero** — the `O98` shape one layer down, where the empty result is a fact about which table you asked rather than about the world.

- **Normalized reconciliations model is dead schema. DESIGNATED: ROADMAP §0 TIER 3 "normalized reconciliations model".** `reconciliations` was created (`005`) in a normalized shape (`bank_account_id`, `statement_date`, `statement_ending_balance`) with a child `reconciliation_items` table. The app never adopted it — ReconView and the bank-upload flow read/write a **denormalized** record instead (`account_id`, `statement_balance`, `books_balance`, `difference`, and jsonb arrays of matched/unmatched transactions). Migration `035` added those denormalized columns and relaxed the three unused NOT-NULLs so the insert succeeds. The normalized columns and `reconciliation_items` remain but are unused. **Future consideration (not now):** migrate reconciliation records to the proper normalized relational model (`reconciliations` header + `reconciliation_items` lines, FK to `journal_entry_lines`) for audit-grade record-keeping. This is a deliberate, separate project — the denormalized shape is the working source of truth today.
- **`ap_invoices` — RESOLVED (dropped in migration `045_drop_ap_invoices.sql`, applied 2026-06-26; C78).** It was a fully-structured parallel AP-invoice model that zero app code referenced (payables are booked to `journal_entries`); the table and the dead `ap_aging` view were confirmed empty/unreferenced and dropped. *(This entry previously read "left in place" — stale, corrected by the orphan audit.)*
- **Schema is now reproducible from the repo (resolved).** Previously the migrations could not rebuild from empty — ~21 core tables predate `001` with no `create table` DDL, and `001` assumes they exist. Fixed by `000_baseline_schema.sql` (a schema-only `pg_dump` of live): **empty DB → `000` → `001…036` reproduces the live schema.** Caveats: `000` is for a fresh database only (bare `CREATE TABLE`; do not run on production); it targets a fresh **Supabase** project (relies on the `auth`/`storage`/`extensions` schemas and `auth.uid()`); and a trigger on `auth.users` that syncs `public.users` lives outside `public` and is not captured by the dump.
  - **DECLINED 2026-08-11 — the `auth.users` trigger gap stays documented, not fixed. No row, no work.** Reason: it is a **provable no-op for the stated purpose** — `000` exists to rebuild a DEVELOPMENT database from empty, and a fresh Supabase project recreates that trigger through normal signup wiring; the gap has never once been hit, because nobody has rebuilt production from `000` and nobody should (`000` is fresh-database-only by design). Capturing cross-schema objects would mean owning a `pg_dump` of `auth`, which is Supabase's schema to change under us. **The honest caveat above IS the deliverable.** *(Noted while here: the sentence says `000` → `001…036`, while the migrations ledger runs through `061` — the rebuild claim is scoped to the baseline chain, not to current live.)*
- **Function divergence from missing migrations (resolved by `036`).** Five SECURITY DEFINER functions — `is_company_member` (the platform-admin/Support-Mode bypass), `post_journal_entry`, `seed_company_accounts`, `security_check`, `list_company_members` — were last modified live by the un-committed `016/017/020/025`, so the committed chain holds older bodies. On a rebuild, the older `create or replace` would clobber the live version. `036_reassert_baseline_functions.sql` re-asserts the live bodies verbatim (a no-op on production), so a rebuild terminates exactly at live. **Lesson:** absence of a migration file does not imply absence live (e.g. `notifications` came from an un-committed `024`); verify against `information_schema`, never infer from the repo alone.

## 12. GAAP correctness as tested code

Every economic event's journal entry must be a **pure `buildXEntry()` in a lib module with a unit test asserting exact Dr/Cr/amount, routed through the handler — never built inline in a view or by the AI.** `tests/gaapInvariants.test.js` is the living spec/guardrail: over a fixture of every event it asserts (a) Dr = Cr, (b) Assets = Liabilities + Equity + NetIncome, (c) payments/collections/opening/reversals leave net income unchanged, (d) only 4xxx/5–8xxx move net income. The AI may **reason and explain** with GAAP knowledge but **only executes whitelisted actions** (`aiCapabilities.js`); it never constructs raw entries.

**The 17-event inventory** (status tracked in the guardrail/remediation): 1 book bill, 2 pay bill (✅ Step 1), 3 direct-to-cash, 4 issue invoice, 5 collect (✅), 6 opening balances, 7 bank/cash opening, 8 depreciation, 9 prepaid capitalize+amortize, 10 accrued liabilities, 11 deferred-revenue receipt+recognition, 12 lease (ASC 842), 13 payroll, 14 void→reversing entry, 15 reversal, 16 sales tax, **17 year-end closing** (Dr each Revenue / Cr each Expense / plug net to Retained Earnings 3100; zeroes the period's P&L; new year starts at zero).

**Fiscal-year / Retained-Earnings model (built — derived soft close, Option A):** `reports.js` `fiscalYearSplit(invoices, {asOf, fiscalYearEnd, cutoffDate})` (+ pure `fiscalYearStart`) splits all-time net income into prior-fiscal-years' closed net (→ **beginning Retained Earnings**, always shown as a distinct line) and the current FY's net (→ **Net Income (current period)**), keyed off `companies.fiscal_year_end` and floored at `cutoff_date`. The Balance Sheet shows the two as separate lines summing to Total RE; `priorNet + currentNet === all-time net`, so every total and the balance check are unchanged — only the RE split. This fixes the pre-fix bug where a prior-year (e.g. 2024) loss was mislabeled "current period" and the Balance-Sheet net income diverged from the Income Statement from a client's 2nd fiscal year on. Guardrail: `gaapInvariants.test.js` "fiscal-year RE split" asserts prior+current=all-time, the year-boundary case (prior loss → beginning RE, not current), FYE handling, and cutoff flooring. No posted closing entries required (that's the deferred hard close, #17).

### Governing principles (conversion & cash)

- **Clean cutoff date ("Day One").** Each company has one `companies.cutoff_date`. The entire starting position is **one balanced opening journal entry** as of the cutoff — the prior trial balance (assets, liabilities, already-accumulated retained earnings), plugging the residual to **Opening Balance Equity (3400)**. **No transaction may be dated before the cutoff**; pre-cutoff activity is represented solely by opening balances. This makes the retained-earnings double-count structurally impossible. Enforcement is **hybrid**: `persistJournalEntry` hard-rejects pre-cutoff bookings with a redirect ("record it by adjusting your opening balances"); imports skip pre-cutoff rows; posting opening balances is hard-blocked if live pre-cutoff transactions exist (`preCutoffActivity`); legacy companies with no cutoff get no enforcement (warn-only). The cutoff is editable until the opening entry is posted, then **locked**. Opening balances post through the canonical path (`buildOpeningBalanceEntry` → `post_journal_entry`, `source='opening_balance'`) **and** persist to the `opening_balances` table (`journal_entry_id`, `posted=true`); `loadAllData` reads them back so they survive refresh. Editing = reverse/replace (soft-delete the prior opening JE + rows, repost) — never duplicate.
- **Bank-as-source-of-truth.** A bank account's `current_balance` IS the opening balance of that bank's cash GL account. It flows through the **same** opening entry (Dr that bank's cash GL / Cr OBE), keyed by GL account; bank-linked cash accounts are shown **read-only** in the opening-balances grid (valued from the bank), so no GL account is opened twice. A cash balance is never a free-floating field invisible to the GL.
- **The QBO-import model is the same** (item 43): trial balance at the cutoff as the opening entry, then live transactions forward — not bulk pre-cutoff history. Keep them consistent.
- **Cash on hand is GL-derived (single source).** Every displayed cash figure (dashboard card, runway, health score, KPIs, monthly report, AI snapshot) derives from `reports.js` `glCashOnHand(invoices, cashCodes, {asOf})` = Σ `glAccountBalance` over the cash/cash-equivalent accounts (roles `cash`+`savings` + bank-linked GL codes), computed once in `ERP` as `glCash`. So dashboard cash === Balance Sheet cash line === `glAccountBalance(cash)` by construction. `computeCashPosition` and the ad-hoc runway-drill formula were removed. The **bank statement balance** (`bank_accounts.current_balance`) is kept but only as the reconciliation target (what the bank says) — never the displayed cash on hand (what the books say); a difference is a reconciliation item.
- **Parallel P&L derivations are lock-tested, not rewritten (cluster #4).** `computeRevenue/Expenses/NetIncome` and `trialBalance` already re-derive from the ledger (sum of entries), so they were left as-is rather than rewired through `glAccountBalance` (lowest-severity, highest-blast-radius). `reconciliation.test.js` "cluster #4 lock" asserts they EQUAL the `glAccountBalance` derivation (revenue = Σ over 4xxx, expenses = Σ over 5–8xxx, net income, and trial-balance per account), so any future drift between the two derivation paths fails CI. (The previously-known nuance — revenue/expense booked on an entry's *offset* leg would diverge — is now **resolved** by the Phase 0 flatten fix below: the P&L line is always the primary `gl_code`, so `computeRevenue`/`Expenses` and `glAccountBalance` agree even for `Dr A/R · Cr Revenue` and `Dr Deferred Rev · Cr Revenue` entries.)
- **AR mirrors AP (cluster #2, built).** AR Outstanding (Dashboard, ArView, AR aging total) reads `glAccountBalance(accounts_receivable)` — the same single GL source as AP. Collecting an invoice posts `Dr Cash / Cr AR` through the canonical `markBillPaid(side:"ar")`/`buildPaymentEntry`; the flag-only/local-only collection paths were fixed (SendInvoiceView "mark paid", bank auto-match, and `applyMatch` full matches all route through it; partial matches stay flag-only pending a partial-clearing feature). `planArBackfill` + `040_ar_collection_backfill_oneshot.sql` exist and are tested (symmetric to AP `039`) but are a **no-op on current data** (dry-run showed zero candidates) — kept for a future real-client conversion.
- **AP / GL-balance single source (AP Step 2, built).** `reports.js` `glAccountBalance(code, invoices, {asOf})` is THE canonical GL balance of any account (sum of its journal-entry-line movements, normal-balance signed; primary leg + offset leg for simple entries; multi-line `_` rows primary-only). The Balance Sheet (`getBal`), the Payables "Outstanding" total, and the Dashboard AP total all read it, so they reconcile by construction. "Amount owed" = the GL balance of Accounts Payable (not a `payment_status`-flag sum); the flag still drives the per-bill paid/unpaid LISTS for display. NOTE: the AR/AP **aging** report and the AI `computeAP` remain payment_status/due_date views (aging needs them); they fully converge with the GL only after **AP Step 3 (historical backfill)** posts the missing Dr-AP/Cr-Cash entries for bills marked paid before Step 1. So post-Step-2 the GL-based surfaces MATCH each other but aren't yet CORRECT until Step 3.

- **Depreciation (#8, Phase A, built).** Capitalizing a purchase now creates a real `fixed_assets` master + a generated `depreciation_schedule` (migration `041`), replacing the dead `needs_depreciation` flag (which never produced an entry). Capture: the GAAP clarification's capitalize option carries an **AI-suggested useful life** (`suggestUsefulLifeMonths` — buildings 39yr, vehicles/tech 5yr, furniture/equipment 7yr, default 5yr), overridable in the confirm step along with **salvage (default $0)** and **in-service date (defaults to purchase date)**. `createFixedAssetWithSchedule` inserts the asset + pending monthly rows from `buildDepreciationSchedule` (straight-line; last month absorbs rounding so Σ === cost − salvage exactly). **"Run depreciation through DATE"** (ReportsView control → `runDepreciationThrough`) posts every pending row due on/before the date as `Dr 6900 / Cr 1510` through the canonical multi-line path, stamps it `posted` + links the JE, and **auto-flips the asset to `fully_depreciated`** once its last pending row posts (`planDepreciationRun` decides due-rows + flips, purely). Idempotent (only pending rows). Lib `src/lib/depreciation.js`; pure builders + the run-planner are unit-tested (`tests/depreciation.test.js`: suggestion, schedule sums-to-base, periodic posting, fully-depreciated flip) and `buildDepreciationEntry` is wired into the gaapInvariants guardrail (#8). Deferred variants (declining-balance/units-of-production/MACRS) noted below.
- **Canonical multi-line write path (Phase 0, built).** Multi-line economic events (deferred-revenue recognition #11b, prepaid amortization #9, lease commencement #12, and going forward payroll #13 + sales-tax invoices #16) post as **ONE balanced journal entry with N lines** through `buildJournalEntry({lines})` (`src/lib/journalEntries.js`, pure + tested) → `persistMultiLineEntry` (`App.jsx`, resolves codes→ids and calls `post_journal_entry` once). This **replaces the per-line expansion** in `postContractEntry`/`postAllContractEntries` that posted each line as its own 2-line JE — an active **double-count** bug (a 2-line recognition entry posted revenue on both a primary and an offset leg → 2× revenue). Contract posting now builds one entry per contract JE, refuses unbalanced entries, and reloads to reflect the single posted entry. Paired fix: `flattenJournalEntries` now picks the **P&L line as primary `gl_code`** for simple 2-line entries (was first-debit), so revenue/expense never strands on the offset leg — this is what makes `computeRevenue`/`Expenses` agree with `glAccountBalance` (and repairs the latent post-reload bug where `Dr A/R · Cr Revenue` flattened with A/R as primary). Lock: `tests/multiLineEntry.test.js` runs the **real** `flattenJournalEntries`→derivation path and asserts on a deferred-revenue fixture that `computeRevenue === glAccountBalance('4200') === 500` (the agreement) and that posting once yields 1× not 2× (no double-count); `tests/journalEntries.test.js` covers the builder (balance, N-line, zero-line drop, cent rounding, unbalanced→refused).

**Standard variants deliberately deferred (industry-standard common case built first; full variants are known, not silently missing):**
- **Sales tax (#16):** built = single blended rate on outgoing AR invoices, `Dr A/R / Cr Revenue / Cr Sales Tax Payable (2350)` (sales tax is a liability, never revenue). **✅ PER-LINE RATES AND A JURISDICTION BREAKDOWN ADDED 2026-08-30 (C267)** — `computeLineTax` + `buildArInvoiceEntryPerLine`. The single-rate builder is unchanged and still the default. **The universal-upload sales path remains deferred.**
  - **★★★ THE FAILURE A BLENDED RATE PRODUCES IS THE DANGEROUS KIND: THE INVOICE TOTAL CAN BE RIGHT WHILE THE SPLIT IS WRONG — AND THE SPLIT IS WHAT GETS FILED.** You remit to a jurisdiction, not to an average. **An invoice collecting the correct $86.25 and attributing it to the wrong state is a correct-looking document and a wrong return.**
  - **★★ THE BREAKDOWN IS CARRIED, NOT COLLAPSED.** The GL still credits ONE Sales Tax Payable — a liability account per state needs a migration and is a bigger decision — but the per-jurisdiction amounts ride in the entry's meta, **so a return can be prepared FROM THE BOOKS rather than reconstructed from invoices. Collapsing them at booking time destroys the only place that fact exists.** A test asserts the breakdown always sums to the tax charged.
  - **★★★ EXEMPT, ZERO-RATED AND MISSING ARE THREE DIFFERENT THINGS.** An exempt line (a restaurant's grocery items) is a **decision someone made** and contributes nothing. A stated **0%** is honoured. **A MISSING rate refuses the whole invoice** — treating it as zero would under-collect silently and produce an invoice that looks complete. **The one thing worse than asking is guessing.**
  - **★ THE TOTAL IS UNCHANGED BY DESIGN**, because `C241`'s accuracy control cross-foots *"tax charged"* against the Sales-Tax-Payable balance — **adding jurisdictions must not quietly move the figure that check compares.**
- **Depreciation (#8):** built = **straight-line** monthly schedule, `Dr Depreciation Expense (6900) / Cr Accumulated Depreciation (1510)`. **✅ DECLINING-BALANCE AND UNITS-OF-PRODUCTION ADDED 2026-08-30 (C265)** — pure curves in `depreciationAmounts`, straight-line unchanged and still the default. **MACRS and mid-year conventions remain deferred** (they are tax-table lookups, not curves, and belong with tax work rather than here).
  - **★★★ THE INVARIANT THAT MAKES A METHOD SAFE TO ADD: every schedule sums EXACTLY to cost − salvage.** An asset must be written down to its salvage value and never past it, whatever curve it takes. **Over-depreciating writes off value the business still owns; under-depreciating leaves a stub nobody notices.** Six method/base/period combinations assert it to the cent, including bases that do not divide evenly.
  - **★★ DECLINING BALANCE SWITCHES TO STRAIGHT-LINE, AND THE SWITCH IS NOT AN OPTIMISATION — WITHOUT IT THE ASSET NEVER FINISHES.** The method takes a fraction of what is LEFT each period, so it approaches zero and never arrives; the schedule would end with a stub still on the books. The flat tail (`4000 2400 1440 1080 1080`) **is** the switch, visible in the numbers.
  - **★★ UNITS-OF-PRODUCTION REFUSES WITHOUT THE USAGE RATHER THAN FALLING BACK.** The method exists to measure actual use; a straight-line fallback would produce a schedule that **looks like usage-based depreciation and is not — a wrong answer wearing the right label.**
  - **★ AND AN UNRECOGNISED METHOD FALLS BACK TO STRAIGHT-LINE, pinned to the LITERAL curve.** The first version of that test compared the unknown method to `STRAIGHT_LINE`'s output — **both moved together under a mutation and it passed.** Two sides of one contract checked against each other is the `·3a` shape: it proves they agree, never that either is right.
- **Payroll (#13):** built = standard multi-line `Dr Salaries (gross) / Dr Payroll Tax Expense (employer) / Cr Cash (net pay) / Cr Payroll Taxes Payable (withholdings + employer)`, net pay → Cash (assumes already disbursed). **✅ THE ACCRUE-THEN-PAY TWO-STEP ADDED 2026-08-30 (C266)** — `buildPayrollAccrualEntry` + `buildPayrollDisbursementEntry`. The one-step case is unchanged and still the default; the split matters **whenever the pay period ENDS in one month and the money leaves in the next** — the expense belongs to the period worked, the cash to the day it moved.
  - **★★★ THE ONLY INVARIANT WORTH TESTING HARD: THE TWO ENTRIES TOGETHER ARE EXACTLY THE ONE-STEP ENTRY.** Same expense, same liability, same cash, same totals, and the intermediate liability **closes to zero**. If they differed, a company would get **a different P&L for choosing a different bookkeeping convention on identical facts — not a variant, a bug with a preference setting.**
  - **★★ THE DISBURSEMENT TOUCHES NO EXPENSE ACCOUNT, and a test asserts no 4xxx–8xxx line can appear in it.** If it could move net income the expense would land **twice** — once accrued, once paid — and the P&L would be wrong by a full payroll in the month the money left.
  - **★ THE ACCRUAL REFUSES WITHOUT A LIABILITY ACCOUNT rather than falling back to cash.** A fallback would silently produce the ONE-step entry under a TWO-step name, and the caller would believe the expense and the cash had been separated when they had not.
  - **★★★ AND THE FIXTURE HAD TO GROW AWKWARD NUMBERS, BECAUSE A ROUNDING MUTATION SURVIVED THE ROUND ONES.** Flooring the accrual's net pay left every assertion green — 3150 is already whole. **Rounding is precisely where two independent paths diverge**, so the invariant is now re-asserted on `4133.33 / 316.21 / 3211.07` and three more, including a 3-cent run. *(Also recorded: an earlier mutation reported "no tests" rather than red — it referenced a parameter that does not exist and broke the module. **A module that fails to load is neither a pass nor a fail, and must not be counted as either.**)*

**✅ #17's BUILDER EXISTS AS OF 2026-08-30 (C268) — `buildYearEndCloseEntry`, tested and in the `gaapInvariants` guardrail. It has NO CALLER, deliberately.** §12's rule is that every one of the seventeen events has a pure builder with a test asserting exact Dr/Cr, and this was **the only one without** — so the hard-close decision can now be made without also being a build.
  - **★★ IT MUST NOT BE WIRED WITHOUT THAT DECISION, and the reason sits in the file where someone would wire it: posting a close is not reversible by re-running it, and a second close on the same year would DOUBLE the roll into Retained Earnings while every individual entry still balanced.** A test asserts it is genuinely uncalled — **if that fails, someone wired it, and that is the moment the decision was needed.**
  - **★★★ A CLOSE MUST NEVER TOUCH A BALANCE-SHEET ACCOUNT**, so passing a whole trial balance in is safe: an asset or liability line would move a balance that is supposed to CARRY into the new year — the one thing a close must not do.
  - **★ AND A MUTATION SURVIVED, LOCATING THE REAL GUARANTEE:** making the plug unconditional still produced no line for an even year, **because `buildJournalEntry` drops zero-amount lines**. The protection is doubled, and *"my branch prevents this"* was not the whole truth — recorded so a future reader changing the sign logic knows the second net exists.

**Deferred / specified-but-not-built:** **hard close** (actually posting #17 closing entries + period locking) — build when period-locking becomes a real need; pairs with reconciliation locking. The soft-close derivation is the correctness fix; hard-close is the future locking feature. *(Note 2026-08-30: `078` now gives the DATABASE a period guard, so the locking half of this has effectively arrived from a different direction — the remaining question is whether closing ENTRIES should be posted at all, which is an accounting decision, not a mechanism one.)*

**✅ SHIPPED — Reversal display marker (C207/C215).** The original now reads **"Removed · DATE"** and its control is DISABLED rather than refusing on click. *(This list said "deferred" until 2026-08-30; it shipped the day the `O95` marker was fixed, since the marker is what made it possible.)*

**✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (C262) — "Redo opening setup".** ★★★ **AND IT UNLOCKS RATHER THAN REVERSING, WHICH IS A DEPARTURE FROM WHAT THIS LINE SPECIFIED.** The spec said *"reverses the posted opening entry and unlocks the cutoff"* — but reversing NOW leaves the books with **no starting position in between**: every report in that window wrong in a new way, and permanently so if the person walks away mid-repair. `postOpeningBalances` already supersedes the previous entry when a new one is posted, so **the old position stays live and coherent until a better one replaces it**, and the confirmation says exactly that (*"nothing is removed now"*) — because *"are you sure?"* tells someone nothing, and what they need to know is that their books do not go blank. **A signed month refuses outright** and names whose call it is: the cutoff lock is ours to lift, an accountant's signature is not, and `078` would refuse the write anyway — unlocking would hand someone a thing they then could not change. The control sits **beside the padlock it lifts** (the `O129` lesson), behind a confirm.

**Remediation order:** #14 void→reversal → #6/#7 opening + bank (with the cutoff model that prevents RE double-count) → fiscal-year RE split + #17 + multi-year guardrail assertions → AP Step 2 (canonical `glAccountBalance`) + Step 3 (backfill) → **Phase 0 canonical multi-line write path (built)** → **Phase A missing events** (#8 depreciation straight-line ✅ built, #11 deferred-revenue receipt, #16 sales tax single-rate) → **Phase B shape-extract inline builders** (#4 AR issue, #9 prepaid, #13 payroll net-to-Cash incl. fixing payroll's never-persisted `setInvoices`-only bug, #11b recognition / #12 lease rerouted through the multi-line path). Each event = pure `buildXEntry()` + unit test asserting exact Dr/Cr, validated against `gaapInvariants`, routed through the handler.
